# A Response Calculus for Liouville Brownian Motion I: Simple Spectrum, Joint Eigenvalue Densities, and Ward Identities

**arXiv ID**: 2608.02459v1
**Authors**: Chunhao Cai
**Published**: 2026-08-03
**Categories**: math.PR, math-ph, math.SP
**HTML URL**: https://arxiv.org/html/2608.02459v1

## Abstract

We develop a response calculus for Dirichlet Liouville Brownian motion under Cameron--Martin shifts of the Gaussian free field. On every bounded connected planar domain, without boundary regularity assumptions, we prove throughout the full subcritical range $0<γ<2$ that the generator has almost surely simple spectrum and that every finite vector of ordered eigenvalues has an absolutely continuous law. This resolves the open simple-spectrum problem for Dirichlet Liouville Brownian motion.   The calculus combines coherent versions of Gaussian multiplicative chaos with fixed-space perturbation of the associated trace forms. The first variation of an isolated eigenvalue cluster becomes a finite-dimensional compression on its eigenspace. Every multiple cluster admits a smooth direction with simple first-order splitting, while the response measures of distinct simple eigenvalues are linearly independent. Analytic zero-set and submersion arguments on finite-dimensional Gaussian slices then give the spectral conclusions.   At the operator level, in the same full subcritical range, the calculus yields differentiability of the resolvent in a fixed energy space and tested one-sided, moving-measure, and fixed-base resolvent Ward identities. We construct explicit causal tempered distributions whose Laplace transforms realize the moving-measure and fixed-base responses; no time-domain heat-semigroup difference-quotient convergence is asserted. Finally, for $0<γ<\sqrt2$, Green--Riesz potentials of response measures for spatially averaged resolvent observables realize their Gaussian Sobolev gradients. This yields absolute continuity for the occupation resolvent at every parameter and, outside deterministic null sets of resolvent parameters, for further scalar observables and finite families associated with disjoint non-negative test functions.

## A Response Calculus for Liouville Brownian Motion I:
Simple Spectrum, Joint Eigenvalue Densities, and Ward IdentitiesChunhao Cai
School of Mathematics (Zhuhai), Sun Yat-sen University
caichh9@mail.sysu.edu.cn

## Abstract

We develop a response calculus for Dirichlet Liouville Brownian motion under
Cameron–Martin shifts of the Gaussian free field. On every bounded connected
planar domain, without boundary regularity assumptions, we prove throughout the
full subcritical range0<γ<20<\gamma<2that the generator has almost surely simple
spectrum and that every finite vector of ordered eigenvalues has an absolutely
continuous law. This resolves the open simple-spectrum problem for Dirichlet
Liouville Brownian motion.

The calculus combines coherent versions of Gaussian multiplicative chaos with
fixed-space perturbation of the associated trace forms. The first variation of
an isolated eigenvalue cluster becomes a finite-dimensional compression on its
eigenspace. Every multiple cluster admits a smooth direction with simple
first-order splitting, while the response measures of distinct simple
eigenvalues are linearly independent. Analytic zero-set and submersion
arguments on finite-dimensional Gaussian slices then give the spectral
conclusions.

At the operator level, in the same full subcritical range, the calculus yields
differentiability
of the resolvent in a fixed energy space and tested one-sided, moving-measure,
and fixed-base resolvent Ward identities. We construct explicit causal
tempered distributions whose Laplace transforms realize the moving-measure and
fixed-base responses; no time-domain heat-semigroup difference-quotient
convergence is asserted. Finally, for0<γ<20<\gamma<\sqrt{2}, Green–Riesz
potentials of response measures for spatially averaged resolvent observables
realize their Gaussian Sobolev gradients. This yields absolute continuity for
the occupation resolvent at every parameter and, outside deterministic null
sets of resolvent parameters, for further scalar observables and finite
families associated with disjoint non-negative test functions.

2020 Mathematics Subject Classification.Primary 60J35; Secondary 47A10, 60G57, 31C25.

Keywords and phrases.Liouville Brownian motion; Gaussian multiplicative chaos; simple spectrum;
joint eigenvalue densities; response calculus; resolvent Ward identities.

## 1Introduction

Liouville Brownian motion (LBM) is the diffusion obtained by time-changing planar
Brownian motion with the Liouville quantum gravity area measure. Its construction
from Gaussian multiplicative chaos (GMC), and the associated Dirichlet form, heat
kernel, and resolvent, were developed in[1,13,14,5,17]. Recent work on the
spectral geometry of LQG establishes Weyl asymptotics and heat-trace asymptotics[2,4,3].
Such counting results are macroscopic: by themselves, they do not decide whether two individual
eigenvalues can coincide, or whether finite collections of indexed eigenvalues have
joint densities. In fact, simple spectrum for the Dirichlet LBM generator is posed
explicitly as an open problem in[2, Problem 4.2].

Related deterministic work proves residual simplicity under bounded potential
perturbations[10]and generic linear
independence of squared Dirichlet eigenfunctions under domain deformations[19]. Neither framework directly yields the results
below: both conclusions are generic in the sense of Baire category rather than
holding almost surely under a Gaussian law,
while the Liouville perturbation changes a singular speed measure, hence also the
ambientL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h})space. The present conclusions also include joint absolute
continuity of every finite ordered eigenvalue vector.

The paper develops a Cameron–Martin response calculus for the Liouville speed
measure and its trace forms, realized by spectral compression and by
full-resolvent differentiation. In both, Green potentials represent the
measure-valued response in the Cameron–Martin space.
The first is spectral non-degeneracy throughout the subcritical regime. For
every bounded connected planar domain, regardless of boundary regularity, the
Dirichlet LBM spectrum is almost surely simple, and every finite vector of
ordered eigenvalues is absolutely continuous. The extension to rough domains
rests on local comparison of Dirichlet chaos, localization of full
quasi-support, and heat-kernel domination; the subsequent transversality
argument is local. At the full-resolvent level, the same calculus gives
fixed-energy resolvent
response, tested resolvent Ward identities, and explicit causal
Laplace-transform representatives of the moving-measure and fixed-base
responses for0<γ<20<\gamma<2, together with density results for spatially averaged resolvent observables when0<γ<20<\gamma<\sqrt{2}. Gaussian Sobolev closure is used only for the last density
argument, where the narrower range comes from annealed second-order estimates.

The individual ingredients at the ends of this construction are classical:
Cameron–Martin covariance is part of the abstract characterization of
subcritical GMC[21], and a measurable GMC map with a simultaneous
shift identity is available on closed even-dimensional manifolds[11, Theorem 4.1]. Finite-dimensional Gaussian
product decomposition is standard in abstract Wiener space[6], and the trace-form and analytic-perturbation inputs come from[12,16]. The framework is the
version-sensitive synthesis needed by both applications: coherent GMC families
on Gaussian fibers and transport of varying trace forms to one Hilbert space.
Its finite compressions drive Part II; its closed resolvent derivative drives
Part III.

## 1.1The model and main results

## The Dirichlet LBM generator.

LetD⊂ℝ2D\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}be bounded and connected; no regularity of∂D\partial Dis assumed. Lethhbe the
zero-boundary GFF onDDwith lawℙ\mathbb{P}, and fix a subcritical parameter0<γ<20<\gamma<2. WriteMh=MhcanM_{h}=M_{h}^{\rm can}for the full-variance GMC measure
specified in (3.1). The
Cameron–Martin space and Dirichlet energy areℋ=H01​(D),ℰ​(u,v)=12​π​∫D∇u⋅∇v​d​x,⟨u,v⟩ℋ=ℰ​(u,v).\mathcal{H}=H_{0}^{1}(D),\qquad\mathcal{E}(u,v)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{D}\nabla u\cdot\nabla v\,dx,\qquad\langle u,v\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{E}(u,v).(1.1)

The Liouville clock construction and Revuz identification[1,14], combined with the
interior GFF decomposition and localization in
Lemma3.3, give, almost surely,Mh​(D)<∞,Mh​is smooth,q​-​suppℰ⁡Mh=D.M_{h}(D)<\infty,\qquad M_{h}\text{ is smooth},\qquad\operatorname{q\text{-}supp}_{\mathcal{E}}M_{h}=D.

Hence the killed time-change theorem[12, Theorem 6.2.1 and equation (6.2.22)]yields the closed densely defined form(ℰ,ℱh)(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}_{h})onL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h})withℱh={u∈H01​(D):u~∈L2​(Mh)},\mathcal{F}_{h}=\{u\in H_{0}^{1}(D):\widetilde{u}\in L^{2}(M_{h})\},(1.2)

whereu~\widetilde{u}is the quasi-continuous representative; see also[14, Theorem 1.7]. LetAhA_{h}be the associated
non-negative self-adjoint operator.

Compactness and discreteness are known in the standard LBM settings; the
rough-domain measurable realization used here is recorded in
Theorem5.1. The new assertions in the next
theorem are simplicity and joint absolute continuity.

## Theorem 1.1.

Assume0<γ<20<\gamma<2. Almost surely,AhA_{h}has compact resolvent and its
eigenvalues, ordered with multiplicity, satisfy0<Λ1h<Λ2h<⋯↑∞.0<\Lambda_{1}^{h}<\Lambda_{2}^{h}<\cdots\uparrow\infty.(1.3)

Moreover, for everym≥1m\geq 1and every1≤n1<⋯<nm1\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{m},(Λn1h,…,Λnmh)#​ℙ≪ℒm.(\Lambda_{n_{1}}^{h},\ldots,\Lambda_{n_{m}}^{h})_{\#}\mathbb{P}\ll\mathcal{L}^{m}.(1.4)

The simplicity and joint-density conclusions also hold for the singular-scale LQG normalization used in[2, (2.1)]; see
Corollary7.10. In particular, its simplicity
assertion answers[2, Problem 4.2]for Dirichlet
GFF/LBM on arbitrary bounded connected planar domains without boundary
regularity assumptions. The joint-density conclusion is a further
result; we are not aware of a previous joint absolute-continuity theorem of this
form for Dirichlet LBM.

The proof of Theorem1.1separates a pathwise
perturbative statement from a finite-dimensional probabilistic argument. After
transportingL2​(eγ​τ​f​Mh)L^{2}(e^{\gamma\tau f}M_{h})toL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h}), analytic form perturbation reduces
the first variation of an isolated cluster atΛ\Lambdato the finite matrix−γ​Λ​(∫Df​ϕi​ϕj​𝑑Mh)1≤i,j≤r.-\gamma\Lambda\left(\int_{D}f\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\,dM_{h}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}.

Hereϕ1,…,ϕr\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{r}is anL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h})-orthonormal basis of the cluster
eigenspace. For a multiple cluster, we produce a smooth direction for which
this matrix has simple spectrum. For distinct simple
eigenvalues, the signed response measures−γ​Λnh​(ϕnh)2​Mh-\gamma\Lambda_{n}^{h}(\phi_{n}^{h})^{2}M_{h}are linearly independent; the key input is
the decomposition of the distributional equation for(ϕnh)2(\phi_{n}^{h})^{2}into itsMhM_{h}-singular and Lebesgue-absolutely-continuous parts. Disintegration of the
GFF along finitely many such Cameron–Martin directions, combined with analytic
zero-set arguments, local submersions, and countable localization, then gives
simplicity and joint absolute continuity.

The pathwise spectral argument covers the full range0<γ<20<\gamma<2; the narrower
range for averaged-resolvent densities is explained in
Remark2.1.

## Tested Ward identities.

Fixf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}, putMτ=Mh,τf,cohM_{\tau}=M_{h,\tau}^{f,\mathrm{coh}}, and setsτ=e−γ​τ​fs_{\tau}=e^{-\gamma\tau f}. Fora,b∈ℬb​(D)a,b\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D), the operator pairings
used below are𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=∫Da​Rλh+τ​f​b​𝑑Mτ,\displaystyle=\int_{D}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h+\tau f}b\,dM_{\tau},(1.10)𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=𝒬λ,τ​(a​sτ,b​sτ).\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(as_{\tau},bs_{\tau}).(1.11)

In kernel form, the first pairing moves both endpoint measures, while the second
keeps them fixed atMhM_{h}. Indeed, whenever a symmetricMτM_{\tau}-kernelrλh+τ​fr_{\lambda}^{h+\tau f}ofRλh+τ​fR_{\lambda}^{h+\tau f}is chosen,𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)=∬D×Da​(x)​rλh+τ​f​(x,y)​b​(y)​Mh​(d​x)​Mh​(d​y).\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=\iint_{D\times D}a(x)r_{\lambda}^{h+\tau f}(x,y)b(y)\,M_{h}(dx)M_{h}(dy).

Thus the definition of𝒥λ,τ\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}is operator-theoretic and does
not require a simultaneous choice of kernels along the whole parameter line.

## Theorem 1.3.

Let0<γ<20<\gamma<2,f∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}, anda,b∈ℬb​(D)a,b\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D). On a single event
of probability one, the following identities hold for everyλ>0\lambda>0:dd​τ|τ=0​𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=γ​∫Df​(a​Rλh​b+b​Rλh​a−λ​Rλh​a​Rλh​b)​𝑑Mh,\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{D}f\bigl(aR_{\lambda}^{h}b+bR_{\lambda}^{h}a-\lambda R_{\lambda}^{h}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\bigr)\,dM_{h},(1.12)dd​τ|τ=0​𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=−γ​λ​∫Df​Rλh​a​Rλh​b​𝑑Mh.\displaystyle=-\gamma\lambda\int_{D}f\,R_{\lambda}^{h}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\,dM_{h}.(1.13)

LetExt+⁡g\operatorname{Ext}_{+}gdenote the extension by zero tot<0t<0of a function on[0,∞)[0,\infty). Withℭth​(a,b;f)=∫0t∫DPt−sh​a​(z)​f​(z)​Psh​b​(z)​Mh​(d​z)​𝑑s,\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)=\int_{0}^{t}\!\int_{D}P_{t-s}^{h}a(z)f(z)P_{s}^{h}b(z)\,M_{h}(dz)\,ds,(1.14)

define the following causal tempered distributions, with∂t\partial_{t}understood distributionally:𝒲h​(a,b;f)\displaystyle\mathscr{W}^{h}(a,b;f)=γ​𝟏[0,∞)​(t)​∫Df​(a​Pth​b+b​Pth​a)​𝑑Mh−γ​∂t(Ext+⁡ℭh​(a,b;f)),\displaystyle=\gamma\mathbf{1}_{[0,\infty)}(t)\int_{D}f\bigl(aP_{t}^{h}b+bP_{t}^{h}a\bigr)\,dM_{h}-\gamma\partial_{t}\!\left(\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{h}(a,b;f)\right),(1.15)𝒦h​(a,b;f)\displaystyle\mathscr{K}^{h}(a,b;f)=−γ​∂t(Ext+⁡ℭh​(a,b;f)).\displaystyle=-\gamma\partial_{t}\!\left(\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{h}(a,b;f)\right).(1.16)

Writingℒ\mathscr{L}for the Laplace transform, they satisfyℒ​[𝒲h​(a,b;f)]​(λ)=∂τ𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)|τ=0,ℒ​[𝒦h​(a,b;f)]​(λ)=∂τ𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)|τ=0.\mathscr{L}[\mathscr{W}^{h}(a,b;f)](\lambda)=\left.\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)\right|_{\tau=0},\qquad\mathscr{L}[\mathscr{K}^{h}(a,b;f)](\lambda)=\left.\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)\right|_{\tau=0}.(1.17)

The heat formulas are identities of causal tempered distributions at the level of
Laplace transforms; no time-domain difference-quotient convergence is asserted.

## Densities for averaged resolvent observables.

Atτ=0\tau=0the two pairings coincide. Their common value isJλa,b(h):=⟨a,Rλhb⟩h=∫DaRλhbdMh.J_{\lambda}^{a,b}(h):=\langle a,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\rangle_{h}=\int_{D}aR_{\lambda}^{h}b\,dM_{h}.(1.18)

Forf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}and every fixedτ\tau, Cameron–Martin covariance givesJλa,b​(h+τ​f)=𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)almost surely.J_{\lambda}^{a,b}(h+\tau f)=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)\qquad\text{almost surely}.

Thus the field response of the random observableJλa,bJ_{\lambda}^{a,b}is the
moving-measure response. The fixed-base pairing𝒥λ,τ\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}freezes both endpoint measures atMhM_{h}and has a
different response.
Forf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}, the directional response ofJλa,bJ_{\lambda}^{a,b}is represented byσλa,b,h=γ​(a​Rλh​b+b​Rλh​a−λ​Rλh​a​Rλh​b)​Mh,∂fJλa,b=∫Df​𝑑σλa,b,h.\sigma_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}=\gamma\bigl(aR_{\lambda}^{h}b+bR_{\lambda}^{h}a-\lambda R_{\lambda}^{h}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\bigr)M_{h},\qquad\partial_{f}J_{\lambda}^{a,b}=\int_{D}f\,d\sigma_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}.(1.19)

LetGDG_{D}be the Dirichlet Green kernel, let𝒰D​ν\mathscr{U}_{D}\nudenote the finite-energy
potential ofν\nu, and letDGD^{\mathrm{G}}denote the canonical Gaussian Sobolev gradient. If0<γ<20<\gamma<\sqrt{2},
Proposition10.8and
CorollaryA.1giveJλa,b∈Dom​(DG),DG​Jλa,b=𝒰D​σλa,b,h∈ℋ.J_{\lambda}^{a,b}\in\mathrm{Dom}(D^{\mathrm{G}}),\qquad D^{\mathrm{G}}J_{\lambda}^{a,b}=\mathscr{U}_{D}\sigma_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}\in\mathcal{H}.(1.20)

Consequently, in this range, any finite familyJi=Jλiai,biJ_{i}=J_{\lambda_{i}}^{a_{i},b_{i}}with response measuresσih\sigma_{i}^{h}has Gaussian
gradient Gram matrix⟨DG​Ji,DG​Jj⟩ℋ=∬D×DGD​(x,y)​σih​(d​x)​σjh​(d​y).\bigl\langle D^{\mathrm{G}}J_{i},D^{\mathrm{G}}J_{j}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\iint_{D\times D}G_{D}(x,y)\,\sigma_{i}^{h}(dx)\sigma_{j}^{h}(dy).(1.21)

If this matrix is positive definite almost surely, the standard Bouleau–Hirsch
criterion recalled in AppendixAyields absolute
continuity.

## Organization.

Part I develops the pathwise response framework. After fixing the Gaussian
realization and version conventions in Section2,
Section3constructs coherent Cameron–Martin families for
GMC, Section4develops the trace form, Green
potentials, and measurable spectral data, and
Section5places finite-dimensional perturbations
on one Hilbert space. Parts II and III are parallel applications of this
framework. Part II identifies the cluster response and proves its
transversality in Section6;
Section7then combines this with Gaussian slicing to
prove the main spectral theorem. Part III treats resolvent response:
Section8proves the fixed-energy derivative,
Section9derives the Ward identities and their causal
Laplace representatives, and Section10proves the
averaged-resolvent density results. The appendix records the Gaussian Sobolev
realization and the Bouleau–Hirsch criterion.

## Part IPathwise response framework

## 2Gaussian setup, versions, and notationD⊂ℝ2​bounded, open, and connected,γ∈(0,2).D\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\ \text{bounded, open, and connected},\qquad\gamma\in(0,2).(2.1)

## Normalization.

Put𝖫0:=−12​π​ΔD,𝖫0​φk(0)=λk(0)​φk(0),0<λ1(0)≤λ2(0)≤⋯,\mathsf{L}_{0}:=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\Delta_{D},\qquad\mathsf{L}_{0}\varphi_{k}^{(0)}=\lambda_{k}^{(0)}\varphi_{k}^{(0)},\qquad 0<\lambda_{1}^{(0)}\leq\lambda_{2}^{(0)}\leq\cdots,(2.2)

## Versions.

The canonical GMC map𝖷∋x⟼Mxcan∈ℳfin+​(D)\mathsf{X}\ni x\longmapsto M_{x}^{\rm can}\in\mathcal{M}_{\rm fin}^{+}(D)

is used for field measurability, while pathwise perturbations use coherent GMC
families. Their fixed-parameter and fiberwise compatibility is established in
Theorem3.6and
Lemma5.4. Measurable spectral data are
encoded by eigenvalues, finite-rank projections, and traces rather than
eigenfunction signs.

## Probability-one events.

All named full-probability events below are chosen Borel, either by a countable
construction or by the convention (2.9).

## Remark 2.1.

The standing model range is0<γ<20<\gamma<2; every model-dependent statement without a
narrower displayed hypothesis is asserted on this range. With the normalization
(3.1), subcriticalL1L^{1}convergence[15,20]gives, for every BorelA⊂DA\subset D,𝔼​[Mh​(A)]=|A|,0<γ<2.\mathbb{E}[M_{h}(A)]=|A|,\qquad 0<\gamma<2.(2.11)

The restriction0<γ<20<\gamma<\sqrt{2}is imposed explicitly on statements using an
annealed second-order GMC input, in particular𝔼​[Mh​(D)2]<∞,𝔼​ℐD​(Mh)<∞,𝔼​∬D2GD​(x,y)2​Mh​(d​x)​Mh​(d​y)<∞,0<γ<2.\begin{gathered}\mathbb{E}[M_{h}(D)^{2}]<\infty,\qquad\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{I}_{D}(M_{h})<\infty,\\
\mathbb{E}\iint_{D^{2}}G_{D}(x,y)^{2}\,M_{h}(dx)M_{h}(dy)<\infty,\qquad 0<\gamma<\sqrt{2}.\end{gathered}(2.12)

For the concrete averaged-resolvent observables, this second-order restriction is
used only to deduceJλa,b∈L2​(Ω)∩Dom​(DG)and the resulting density statements.J_{\lambda}^{a,b}\in L^{2}(\Omega)\cap\mathrm{Dom}(D^{\mathrm{G}})\quad\text{and the resulting density statements}.

The abstract deterministic and Gaussian Sobolev criteria themselves impose no
additional restriction onγ\gamma.

The constantsc,Cc,Cmay depend on displayed deterministic data; random constants
carry a field subscript. Boundedness and connectedness are used through coercivity,
irreducibility, and the Green and heat-kernel estimates proved below; no estimate
uses a regular boundary trace.

whereΔD≤0\Delta_{D}\leq 0is the Dirichlet Laplacian. Forx∈𝖷x\in\mathsf{X},Mx,ε​(d​z)=exp⁡(γ​𝖰ε​x​(z)−γ22​𝔼​[hε​(z)2])​d​z.M_{x,\varepsilon}(dz)=\exp\!\left(\gamma\mathsf{Q}_{\varepsilon}x(z)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}[h_{\varepsilon}(z)^{2}]\right)dz.(3.1)

If(φℓ)ℓ≥1⊂Cb​(D)(\varphi_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}\subset C_{b}(D)is convergence determining, thenx⟼∫Dφℓ​𝑑Mx,εis Borel for every​(ℓ,ε),x\longmapsto\int_{D}\varphi_{\ell}\,dM_{x,\varepsilon}\quad\text{is Borel for every }(\ell,\varepsilon),

hencex↦Mx,εx\mapsto M_{x,\varepsilon}is Borel.

We shall repeatedly use the following boundary-free approximation. Ifg∈Cc∞​(D)g\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D), theng∈Dom​(ΔD)g\in\mathrm{Dom}(\Delta_{D})and the sub-Markov property of
the killed semigroup gives𝖰t​g−g=∫0t𝖰s​ΔD​g​𝑑s,‖𝖰t​g−g‖∞≤t​‖ΔD​g‖∞.\mathsf{Q}_{t}g-g=\int_{0}^{t}\mathsf{Q}_{s}\Delta_{D}g\,ds,\qquad\|\mathsf{Q}_{t}g-g\|_{\infty}\leq t\|\Delta_{D}g\|_{\infty}.(3.2)

The identity first holds inL2​(D)L^{2}(D)by the semigroup generator theorem. Its
right-hand side belongs toL∞​(D)L^{\infty}(D)and satisfies the displayed estimate,
so it supplies the bounded representative used below. Notice that
(3.2) does not require the
killed semigroup to be strongly continuous onC0​(D)C_{0}(D), a property which can
fail at irregular boundary points.

## 3.1A Borel GMC version and coherent Cameron–Martin lines

Field-measurability and Cameron–Martin covariance are part of the abstract GMC
theory[21, Definition 11 and Corollary 18]. In a different
geometric setting, an explicit measurable map from a negative Sobolev field space
to finite measures, together with a simultaneous shift identity, is given in[11, Theorem 4.1]. Our purpose below is to fix
the killed-heat representative on a rough Dirichlet domain and its pointwise orbit
compatibility, as required by the later fiberwise construction.

## Lemma 3.1.

There exist a deterministic sequenceεj↓0\varepsilon_{j}\downarrow 0, a Borel setΩGMC⊂𝖷\Omega_{\rm GMC}\subset\mathsf{X}withℙ​(h∈ΩGMC)=1\mathbb{P}(h\in\Omega_{\rm GMC})=1, and a Borel
map𝐌can:𝖷⟶ℳfin+​(D)\mathbf{M}^{\rm can}:\mathsf{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{+}(D)(3.3)

such thatMx,εj⟹𝐌can​(x),x∈ΩGMC.M_{x,\varepsilon_{j}}\Longrightarrow\mathbf{M}^{\rm can}(x),\qquad x\in\Omega_{\rm GMC}.(3.4)

Moreover,𝐌can​(h)\mathbf{M}^{\rm can}(h)agrees almost surely with the subcritical GMC
measure.

## Proof.

We first record why the heat-semigroup approximation falls within[21, Theorem 25]. IfqtUq_{t}^{U}is the heat kernel of the normalized
killed semigroupe−t​𝖫0U=et​ΔU/(2​π)e^{-t\mathsf{L}_{0}^{U}}=e^{t\Delta_{U}/(2\pi)}onUUandBBis any
disk containingD¯\overline{D}, then domain
monotonicity and the semigroup property giveKε,D(x,y):=Cov(𝖰εDhD(x),𝖰εDhD(y))=∫4​π​ε∞qtD(x,y)dt≤∫4​π​ε∞qtB(x,y)dt=:Kε,B(x,y).K_{\varepsilon,D}(x,y):=\operatorname{Cov}(\mathsf{Q}_{\varepsilon}^{D}h_{D}(x),\mathsf{Q}_{\varepsilon}^{D}h_{D}(y))=\int_{4\pi\varepsilon}^{\infty}q_{t}^{D}(x,y)\,dt\leq\int_{4\pi\varepsilon}^{\infty}q_{t}^{B}(x,y)\,dt=:K_{\varepsilon,B}(x,y).(3.5)

HenceKε,D→GDK_{\varepsilon,D}\to G_{D}almost everywhere, and therefore in measure onD2D^{2}. Gaussian heat-kernel bounds on the fixed diskBB, together with
(3.5), giveKε,D​(x,y)≤CB+log+⁡1|x−y|∨ε,x,y∈D,0<ε≤1.K_{\varepsilon,D}(x,y)\leq C_{B}+\log^{+}\frac{1}{|x-y|\vee\sqrt{\varepsilon}},\qquad x,y\in D,\quad 0<\varepsilon\leq 1.(3.6)

For every Cameron–Martin vectorg∈ℋg\in\mathcal{H}, strong continuity of the killed
heat semigroup gives𝖰ε​g→g\mathsf{Q}_{\varepsilon}g\to ginL2​(D)L^{2}(D), which is the shift
convergence required in Shamov’s approximation theorem. Finally, compare the
last covariance bound with a fixed exact-scale logarithmic field. Kahane’s
convexity inequality and the subcritical positive-moment estimate[20, Theorems 2.11 and 2.16]give, for somep∈(1,4/γ2)p\in(1,4/\gamma^{2}),sup0<ε≤1𝔼​[Mh,ε​(D)p]<∞.\sup_{0<\varepsilon\leq 1}\mathbb{E}[M_{h,\varepsilon}(D)^{p}]<\infty.

Thus the total masses are uniformly integrable. All hypotheses of[21, Theorem 25]are satisfied, so(Mh,ε)ε>0(M_{h,\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}is Cauchy in probability in(ℳfin+​(D),dw)(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{+}(D),d_{\mathrm{w}})and converges to the subcritical
GMC. This is the only place where the general approximation theorem is used.

Chooseεj↓0\varepsilon_{j}\downarrow 0recursively
so thatℙ​(dw​(Mh,εj+1,Mh,εj)>2−j)≤2−j.\mathbb{P}\!\left(d_{\mathrm{w}}(M_{h,\varepsilon_{j+1}},M_{h,\varepsilon_{j}})>2^{-j}\right)\leq 2^{-j}.

The Borel–Cantelli lemma implies that(Mh,εj)j(M_{h,\varepsilon_{j}})_{j}is almost surely Cauchy.
Equivalently, the Borel setΩGMC:=⋂m≥1⋃N≥1⋂j,k≥N{x∈𝖷:dw​(Mx,εj,Mx,εk)<m−1}\Omega_{\rm GMC}:=\bigcap_{m\geq 1}\bigcup_{N\geq 1}\bigcap_{j,k\geq N}\left\{x\in\mathsf{X}:d_{\mathrm{w}}(M_{x,\varepsilon_{j}},M_{x,\varepsilon_{k}})<m^{-1}\right\}

has full Gaussian measure. Completeness ofdwd_{\mathrm{w}}gives a limit on this
set. Define𝐌can​(x):={limj→∞Mx,εj,x∈ΩGMC,0,x∉ΩGMC.\mathbf{M}^{\rm can}(x):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\lim_{j\to\infty}M_{x,\varepsilon_{j}},&x\in\Omega_{\rm GMC},\\[4.30554pt]
0,&x\notin\Omega_{\rm GMC}.\end{cases}

It is Borel because it is the pointwise limit of Borel metric-space-valued maps on
a Borel convergence set, extended by a constant off that set. Finally, the limit
agrees almost surely with the subcritical GMC limit by uniqueness of limits in
probability.
∎

SetMxcan:=𝐌can​(x)M_{x}^{\rm can}:=\mathbf{M}^{\rm can}(x)and abbreviateMh:=MhcanM_{h}:=M_{h}^{\rm can}.

We next localize this canonical version to smooth interior subdomains in order
to obtain the full-quasi-support input used by the trace form.

LetU⋐DU\Subset Dbe a smooth connected subdomain, and lethDh_{D}andhUh_{U}be
zero-boundary GFFs onDDandUU, respectively. They may be coupled so that,
inUU,hD=hU+𝔥D,U,h_{D}=h_{U}+\mathfrak{h}_{D,U},(3.7)

wherehUh_{U}and𝔥D,U\mathfrak{h}_{D,U}are independent and𝔥D,U\mathfrak{h}_{D,U}is almost surely harmonic. For everyV⋐UV\Subset U,
almost surely,MhDcan|V=ρD,U​MhUcan|V,ρD,U​(z):=exp⁡(γ​𝔥D,U​(z)−γ22​𝔼​[𝔥D,U​(z)2]),M_{h_{D}}^{\rm can}|_{V}=\rho_{D,U}\,M_{h_{U}}^{\rm can}|_{V},\qquad\rho_{D,U}(z):=\exp\!\left(\gamma\mathfrak{h}_{D,U}(z)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{h}_{D,U}(z)^{2}]\right),(3.8)

andρD,U,ρD,U−1∈Cb​(V).\rho_{D,U},\ \rho_{D,U}^{-1}\in C_{b}(V).(3.9)

In particular, the two measures in
(3.8) have the same null sets onVV.

## Proof.

The orthogonal decompositionH01​(D)=H01​(U)⊕{v∈H01​(D):ℰ​(v,ϕ)=0​for every​ϕ∈H01​(U)}H_{0}^{1}(D)=H_{0}^{1}(U)\mathbin{\oplus}\{v\in H_{0}^{1}(D):\mathcal{E}(v,\phi)=0\text{ for every }\phi\in H_{0}^{1}(U)\}(3.10)

gives (3.7); this is the domain
Markov property of the GFF[22, Section 2.6]. Interior elliptic
regularity gives a smooth version of𝔥D,U\mathfrak{h}_{D,U}onUU. Moreover,𝔼​[𝔥D,U​(z)​𝔥D,U​(w)]=GD​(z,w)−GU​(z,w),\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{h}_{D,U}(z)\mathfrak{h}_{D,U}(w)]=G_{D}(z,w)-G_{U}(z,w),(3.11)

whose diagonal is continuous onUU. Hence the exponent in
(3.8) is continuous and bounded onVV, as is its negative.

For any local smooth regularizations of the two independent summands in
(3.7), the renormalized
exponentials factor exactly. Passage to the subcritical limit gives
(3.8). Approximation independence[21, Corollary 18]identifies this limit with the canonical
heat-regularized measures used here.
∎

The local comparison transfers full quasi-support from smooth interior domains
to the original rough domain.

## Lemma 3.3.

LetD⊂ℝ2D\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}be bounded, open, and connected, and let0<γ<20<\gamma<2. Almost surely, the canonical measureMhM_{h}is finite and smooth
for(ℰ,H01​(D))(\mathcal{E},H_{0}^{1}(D))and has fullℰ\mathcal{E}-quasi-support. Equivalently,u∈H01​(D),u~=0Mh​-a.e.⟹u=0ℰ​-q.e. on​D.u\in H_{0}^{1}(D),\qquad\widetilde{u}=0\quad M_{h}\text{-a.e.}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad u=0\quad\mathcal{E}\text{-q.e. on }D.(3.12)

## Proof.

Throughout this proof,Cap1,O\operatorname{Cap}_{1,O}denotes the11-capacity
associated with(12​π​∫O∇u⋅∇v​d​x,H01​(O));\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{O}\nabla u\cdot\nabla v\,dx,\ H_{0}^{1}(O)\right);

forO=ℝ2O=\mathbb{R}^{2}, we useH01​(ℝ2)=H1​(ℝ2)H_{0}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})=H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}). IfO1⊂O2O_{1}\subset O_{2}are open andA⊂O1A\subset O_{1}, zero extension givesCap1,O2⁡(A)≤Cap1,O1⁡(A).\operatorname{Cap}_{1,O_{2}}(A)\leq\operatorname{Cap}_{1,O_{1}}(A).

Conversely, ifK⋐O1K\Subset O_{1}andA⊂KA\subset K, multiplication by a fixed cutoff
which equals one nearKKgivesCap1,O1⁡(A)≤CK,O1,O2​Cap1,O2⁡(A).\operatorname{Cap}_{1,O_{1}}(A)\leq C_{K,O_{1},O_{2}}\operatorname{Cap}_{1,O_{2}}(A).

Thus polarity is preserved in both directions after localization to a compact
subset. These are the capacity comparisons used below.

We first prove the assertion on a diskUU. Fixm>0m>0and set𝖫0ℝ2:=−12​π​Δℝ2.\mathsf{L}_{0}^{\mathbb{R}^{2}}:=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

its logarithmic singularity has the same normalization as that ofhUh_{U}.
All GMC measures in this proof use the full-variance normalization of
(3.1). LetMX(m)M_{X^{(m)}}be the GMC ofX(m)X^{(m)}. By[14, Lemmas 1.5–1.6], its Liouville clock is a
strict positive continuous additive functional (PCAF) with supportℝ2\mathbb{R}^{2}and Revuz measureMX(m)M_{X^{(m)}}.
The PCAF support criterion[9, Theorems 3.3.5 and 5.2.1(i)]therefore givesv∈H1​(ℝ2),v~=0MX(m)​-a.e.⟹v=0q.e.v\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\quad\widetilde{v}=0\quad M_{X^{(m)}}\text{-a.e.}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad v=0\quad\text{q.e.}(3.14)

As the Revuz measure of this PCAF,MX(m)M_{X^{(m)}}is smooth. Here full PCAF
support is precisely full quasi-support, equivalently the trace-kernel statement
(3.14).

orthogonal decomposition and the resolvent identity give couplingsX(m)|U\displaystyle X^{(m)}|_{U}=XU(m)+𝔥U(m),\displaystyle=X_{U}^{(m)}+\mathfrak{h}_{U}^{(m)},(3.15)hU\displaystyle h_{U}=XU(m)+YU,Cov⁡(YU)=(𝖫0U)−1−(𝖫0U+m2)−1=m2​(𝖫0U)−1​(𝖫0U+m2)−1.\displaystyle=X_{U}^{(m)}+Y_{U},\qquad\operatorname{Cov}(Y_{U})=(\mathsf{L}_{0}^{U})^{-1}-(\mathsf{L}_{0}^{U}+m^{2})^{-1}=m^{2}(\mathsf{L}_{0}^{U})^{-1}(\mathsf{L}_{0}^{U}+m^{2})^{-1}.(3.16)

the two fields on the left of
(3.15)–(3.16)
have the required marginal laws. The field𝔥U(m)\mathfrak{h}_{U}^{(m)}is
massive-harmonic inUU.
WritingGU(m)G_{U}^{(m)}for the integral kernel of(𝖫0U+m2)−1(\mathsf{L}_{0}^{U}+m^{2})^{-1}, the covariance kernel ofYUY_{U}isCU(m)​(x,y)=m2​∫UGU​(x,z)​GU(m)​(z,y)​𝑑z.C_{U}^{(m)}(x,y)=m^{2}\int_{U}G_{U}(x,z)G_{U}^{(m)}(z,y)\,dz.(3.17)

We also record the relation with the customary singular-scale normalization.

## Remark 3.4.

Forz∈Dz\in D, letHD​(z,z):=limw→z(GD​(z,w)−log⁡1|z−w|),𝔯D​(z):=eHD​(z,z).H_{D}(z,z):=\lim_{w\to z}\left(G_{D}(z,w)-\log\frac{1}{|z-w|}\right),\qquad\mathfrak{r}_{D}(z):=e^{H_{D}(z,z)}.

The Robin radius𝔯D\mathfrak{r}_{D}is continuous and strictly positive onDD,
and is bounded above becauseDDis bounded. WhenDDis simply connected,𝔯D\mathfrak{r}_{D}is the conformal radius. Ifhε∘h_{\varepsilon}^{\circ}denotes the circle
average ofhh, then, forε<dist⁡(z,∂D)\varepsilon<\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial D),𝔼​[(hε∘​(z))2]=log⁡1ε+HD​(z,z).\mathbb{E}[(h_{\varepsilon}^{\circ}(z))^{2}]=\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+H_{D}(z,z).

Consequently, ifMhLQGM_{h}^{\rm LQG}denotes the subcritical limit in probability
of the singular-scale circle-average approximations, then its canonical version
satisfiesMhLQG​(d​z)\displaystyle M_{h}^{\rm LQG}(dz)=𝔯D​(z)γ2/2​Mhcan​(d​z)almost surely.\displaystyle=\mathfrak{r}_{D}(z)^{\gamma^{2}/2}M_{h}^{\rm can}(dz)\qquad\text{almost surely}.(3.27)

Equivalently, it is the limit ofεγ2/2​eγ​hε∘​(z)​d​z\varepsilon^{\gamma^{2}/2}e^{\gamma h_{\varepsilon}^{\circ}(z)}\,dzin probability.
In the simply connected case, the conformal-radius formula is[4, (1.1)]. Approximation independence with the same
reference measure follows from[21, Corollary 18]. Thus the two
conventions are related by a deterministic background weight, rather than
identified with an unchanged reference measure.

We now collect the trace-form properties on a Borel full-probability event.

LetD⊂ℝ2D\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}be bounded and open. There exists a constantCD≥1C_{D}\geq 1such that0≤GD​(x,y)≤CD+log+⁡1|x−y|,x,y∈D.0\leq G_{D}(x,y)\leq C_{D}+\log^{+}\frac{1}{|x-y|},\qquad x,y\in D.(3.44)

y\in D,\ |x-y|\wedge|x^{\prime}-y|\geq r_{\ast}\end{subarray}}|G_{D}(x,y)-G_{D}(x^{\prime},y)|\xrightarrow[\delta\downarrow 0]{}0.(3.45)

This is (3.44).

which implies (3.45).

## Lemma 3.11.

Assume0<γ<20<\gamma<2. There exist a deterministicα=α​(γ)>0\alpha=\alpha(\gamma)>0and a

## 4Liouville forms, Green potentials, and measurable spectral data

We now feed the preceding GMC and Green estimates into the deterministic
trace-form machinery, first for a general smooth measure of full quasi-support.

For a finite positive smooth measureν\nuof full quasi-support andλ>0\lambda>0, setℱν\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\nu}={u∈H01​(D):u~∈L2​(ν)},\displaystyle=\{u\in H_{0}^{1}(D):\widetilde{u}\in L^{2}(\nu)\},aν,λ​(u,v)\displaystyle a_{\nu,\lambda}(u,v)=ℰ​(u,v)+λ​∫Du​v​𝑑ν,‖u‖ν,λ2=aν,λ​(u,u),\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}(u,v)+\lambda\int_{D}uv\,d\nu,\qquad\|u\|_{\nu,\lambda}^{2}=a_{\nu,\lambda}(u,u),(4.1)

andΓℰ​(u,v)=12​π​∇u⋅∇v,Γℰ​(u)=Γℰ​(u,u).\Gamma_{\mathcal{E}}(u,v)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\nabla u\cdot\nabla v,\qquad\Gamma_{\mathcal{E}}(u)=\Gamma_{\mathcal{E}}(u,u).(4.2)

## 4.1Trace forms and deterministic resolvent bounds

## Lemma 4.1.

The trace mapu↦u~u\mapsto\widetilde{u}identifiesℱν\mathcal{F}_{\nu}with a dense subspace ofL2​(D,ν)L^{2}(D,\nu), and(ℱν,∥⋅∥ν,λ)(\mathcal{F}_{\nu},\|\cdot\|_{\nu,\lambda})is a Hilbert space. For
everyψ∈L2​(D,ν)\psi\in L^{2}(D,\nu)there is a uniqueRλν​ψ∈ℱνR_{\lambda}^{\nu}\psi\in\mathcal{F}_{\nu}such
thataν,λ​(Rλν​ψ,v)=∫Dψ​v​𝑑ν,v∈ℱν.a_{\nu,\lambda}(R_{\lambda}^{\nu}\psi,v)=\int_{D}\psi v\,d\nu,\qquad v\in\mathcal{F}_{\nu}.(4.3)

Moreover,‖Rλν​ψ‖ν,λ≤λ−1/2​‖ψ‖L2​(D,ν).\|R_{\lambda}^{\nu}\psi\|_{\nu,\lambda}\leq\lambda^{-1/2}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}.(4.4)

## Proof.

The closedness and density assertion is the full-quasi-support case of the
time-change theorem[12, Theorem 6.2.1 and equation (6.2.22)]. Full
quasi-support supplies the injectivity of the trace map. Consequentlyaν,λa_{\nu,\lambda}is a coercive scalar product onℱν\mathcal{F}_{\nu}. Moreover,|∫Dψ​v​𝑑ν|≤‖ψ‖L2​(D,ν)​‖v‖L2​(D,ν)≤λ−1/2​‖ψ‖L2​(D,ν)​‖v‖ν,λ.\left|\int_{D}\psi v\,d\nu\right|\leq\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}\|v\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}\leq\lambda^{-1/2}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}\|v\|_{\nu,\lambda}.

The Riesz representation theorem gives (4.3); testing
withv=Rλν​ψv=R_{\lambda}^{\nu}\psigives
(4.4).

Forh∈Ωtrh\in\Omega_{\rm tr}, putν=Mh\nu=M_{h}and writeRλh=RλMhR_{\lambda}^{h}=R_{\lambda}^{M_{h}}. Thus(Ah+λ)​u=φ(A_{h}+\lambda)u=\varphimeans preciselyℰ​(u,v)+λ​∫Du​v​𝑑Mh=∫Dφ​v​𝑑Mh,v∈ℱh.\mathcal{E}(u,v)+\lambda\int_{D}uv\,dM_{h}=\int_{D}\varphi v\,dM_{h},\qquad v\in\mathcal{F}_{h}.(4.5)

## 4.2Finite Green-energy measures

Finite-energy potentials provide the Green-kernel realization of the inverse
and of the response functionals used below.

LetGDG_{D}be the non-negative Dirichlet Green kernel associated withℰ\mathcal{E}. For
finite positive Radon measuresμ1\mu_{1}andμ2\mu_{2}onDD, defineℐD​(μ1,μ2):=∬D2GD​(x,y)​μ1​(d​x)​μ2​(d​y)∈[0,∞],ℐD​(μ1):=ℐD​(μ1,μ1).\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}):=\iint_{D^{2}}G_{D}(x,y)\,\mu_{1}(dx)\mu_{2}(dy)\in[0,\infty],\qquad\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1}):=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1},\mu_{1}).

Let𝔐ℰ​(D)\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D)be the class of finite signed Radon measuresν=ν+−ν−\nu=\nu^{+}-\nu^{-}satisfyingℐD​(ν+)+ℐD​(ν−)<∞.\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu^{+})+\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu^{-})<\infty.

Finite-energy-integral theory is standard[12, Section 2.2, especially Theorem 2.2.5]. We identify its
potential with the normalized Dirichlet Green kernel on a rough domain and fix
the representatives used later.

## Lemma 4.5.

Letν1,ν2∈𝔐ℰ​(D)\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D). Each of the four positive mutual energiesℐD​(ν1ε,ν2δ)\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1}^{\varepsilon},\nu_{2}^{\delta}),ε,δ∈{+,−}\varepsilon,\delta\in\{+,-\}, is finite. HenceℐD​(ν1,ν2)\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2}):=ℐD​(ν1+,ν2+)−ℐD​(ν1+,ν2−)−ℐD​(ν1−,ν2+)+ℐD​(ν1−,ν2−),\displaystyle=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1}^{+},\nu_{2}^{+})-\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1}^{+},\nu_{2}^{-})-\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1}^{-},\nu_{2}^{+})+\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1}^{-},\nu_{2}^{-}),ℐD​(νj)\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{j}):=ℐD​(νj,νj),j=1,2,\displaystyle=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j}),\qquad j=1,2,

are well defined without an∞−∞\infty-\inftyconvention. Forj=1,2j=1,2, there is a
unique potential𝒰D​νj∈ℋ\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu_{j}\in\mathcal{H}such thatℰ​(𝒰D​νj,v)=∫Dv~​𝑑νj,v∈ℋ.\mathcal{E}(\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu_{j},v)=\int_{D}\widetilde{v}\,d\nu_{j},\qquad v\in\mathcal{H}.(4.8)

Moreover,ℐD​(ν1,ν2)\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})=ℰ​(𝒰D​ν1,𝒰D​ν2),\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}(\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu_{1},\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu_{2}),(4.9)|ℐD​(ν1,ν2)|\displaystyle|\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})|≤ℐD​(ν1)1/2​ℐD​(ν2)1/2,\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1})^{1/2}\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{2})^{1/2},(4.10)‖𝒰D​ν1‖ℋ2\displaystyle\|\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu_{1}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=ℐD​(ν1),⟨𝒰D​ν1,f⟩ℋ=∫Df~​𝑑ν1,f∈ℋ.\displaystyle=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{1}),\qquad\langle\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu_{1},f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\int_{D}\widetilde{f}\,d\nu_{1},\quad f\in\mathcal{H}.(4.11)

## Proof.

Letμ\mube positive withℐD​(μ)<∞\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)<\infty. The ordinary
Dirichlet heat semigroup isPt(0)=e−t​𝖫0P_{t}^{(0)}=e^{-t\mathsf{L}_{0}}; writeqt(0):=qtDq_{t}^{(0)}:=q_{t}^{D}for its kernel. Its
Dirichlet eigenfunctions have canonical bounded continuous versions inDD:
for anys>0s>0we takeφk(0)​(x):=es​λk(0)​∫Dqs(0)​(x,z)​φk(0)​(z)​𝑑z.\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x):=e^{s\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}\int_{D}q_{s}^{(0)}(x,z)\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(z)\,dz.(4.12)

This representative is independent ofss, because it is continuous inDDand agreesd​xdx-almost everywhere with the original eigenfunction. The killed
heat kernel is dominated by the free heat kernel, and under our
normalization0≤qt(0)​(x,y)≤12​t​exp⁡(−π​|x−y|22​t),qt(0)​(x,x)≤12​t.0\leq q_{t}^{(0)}(x,y)\leq\frac{1}{2t}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\pi|x-y|^{2}}{2t}\right),\qquad q_{t}^{(0)}(x,x)\leq\frac{1}{2t}.(4.13)

Interior elliptic regularity gives continuity, whilee−t​λk(0)​|φk(0)​(x)|2≤qt(0)​(x,x)≤12​te^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}|\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)|^{2}\leq q_{t}^{(0)}(x,x)\leq\frac{1}{2t}(4.14)

gives boundedness. Setak​(μ):=∫Dφk(0)​𝑑μ.a_{k}(\mu):=\int_{D}\varphi_{k}^{(0)}\,d\mu.

For this semigroup, the semigroup identity gives, pointwise onD×DD\times D,qt(0)​(x,y)=∫Dqt/2(0)​(x,z)​qt/2(0)​(z,y)​𝑑z=⟨qt/2(0)​(x,⋅),qt/2(0)​(y,⋅)⟩L2​(D).q_{t}^{(0)}(x,y)=\int_{D}q_{t/2}^{(0)}(x,z)q_{t/2}^{(0)}(z,y)\,dz=\big\langle q_{t/2}^{(0)}(x,\cdot),q_{t/2}^{(0)}(y,\cdot)\big\rangle_{L^{2}(D)}.(4.15)

The two functions in the last inner product belong toL2​(D)L^{2}(D)because their
squared norms areqt(0)​(x,x)q_{t}^{(0)}(x,x)andqt(0)​(y,y)q_{t}^{(0)}(y,y). Their Fourier
coefficients are⟨qt/2(0)​(x,⋅),φk(0)⟩=e−t​λk(0)/2​φk(0)​(x).\big\langle q_{t/2}^{(0)}(x,\cdot),\varphi_{k}^{(0)}\big\rangle=e^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}/2}\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x).

Parseval’s identity in (4.15),
followed by Cauchy–Schwarz and (4.13),
therefore gives the pointwise, absolutely convergent expansionqt(0)​(x,y)=∑k≥1e−t​λk(0)​φk(0)​(x)​φk(0)​(y),∑k≥1e−t​λk(0)​|φk(0)​(x)​φk(0)​(y)|≤12​t.q_{t}^{(0)}(x,y)=\sum_{k\geq 1}e^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(y),\qquad\sum_{k\geq 1}e^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}|\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(y)|\leq\frac{1}{2t}.(4.16)

Fort≥ε>0t\geq\varepsilon>0, the same bound at timeε/2\varepsilon/2gives∑k≥1e−t​λk(0)​|φk(0)​(x)​φk(0)​(y)|≤ε−1​e−(t−ε/2)​λ1(0).\sum_{k\geq 1}e^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}|\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(y)|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}e^{-(t-\varepsilon/2)\lambda_{1}^{(0)}}.

The right-hand side is integrable intt, uniformly in(x,y)(x,y), so termwise
integration givesGε​(x,y):=∫ε∞qt(0)​(x,y)​𝑑t=∑k≥1e−ε​λk(0)λk(0)​φk(0)​(x)​φk(0)​(y).G_{\varepsilon}(x,y):=\int_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}q_{t}^{(0)}(x,y)\,dt=\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{e^{-\varepsilon\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(y).

No boundary continuity is needed for this expansion. In fact, for everyN≥0N\geq 0,supx,y∈D∑k>Ne−ε​λk(0)λk(0)​|φk(0)​(x)​φk(0)​(y)|\displaystyle\sup_{x,y\in D}\sum_{k>N}\frac{e^{-\varepsilon\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}|\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(y)|≤e−ε​λN+1(0)/2λ1(0)​supx,y∈D∑k>Ne−ε​λk(0)/2​|φk(0)​(x)​φk(0)​(y)|\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{e^{-\varepsilon\lambda_{N+1}^{(0)}/2}}{\lambda_{1}^{(0)}}\sup_{x,y\in D}\sum_{k>N}e^{-\varepsilon\lambda_{k}^{(0)}/2}|\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(y)|≤e−ε​λN+1(0)/2λ1(0)​ε.\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{e^{-\varepsilon\lambda_{N+1}^{(0)}/2}}{\lambda_{1}^{(0)}\varepsilon}.(4.17)

Thus the series forGεG_{\varepsilon}converges absolutely and uniformly onD×DD\times D, defines a bounded continuous kernel there, and may be integrated
term-by-term againstμ⊗μ\mu\otimes\mu.
SinceGD​(x,y)=∫0∞qt(0)​(x,y)​𝑑t,Gε​(x,y)=∫ε∞qt(0)​(x,y)​𝑑t,G_{D}(x,y)=\int_{0}^{\infty}q_{t}^{(0)}(x,y)\,dt,\qquad G_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=\int_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}q_{t}^{(0)}(x,y)\,dt,

we have0≤Gε↑GD0\leq G_{\varepsilon}\uparrow G_{D}; henceℐD​(μ)=limε↓0∑k≥1e−ε​λk(0)λk(0)​ak​(μ)2=∑k≥1ak​(μ)2λk(0).\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{e^{-\varepsilon\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}a_{k}(\mu)^{2}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{a_{k}(\mu)^{2}}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}.(4.18)

Consequently,Uμ:=∑k≥1ak​(μ)λk(0)​φk(0)∈H01​(D),ℰ​(Uμ,Uμ)=ℐD​(μ).U_{\mu}:=\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{a_{k}(\mu)}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}\varphi_{k}^{(0)}\in H_{0}^{1}(D),\qquad\mathcal{E}(U_{\mu},U_{\mu})=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu).(4.19)

Forv∈Cc∞​(D)v\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)andvk=⟨v,φk(0)⟩L2​(d​x)v_{k}=\langle v,\varphi_{k}^{(0)}\rangle_{L^{2}(dx)},
the heat-kernel expansion is uniformly convergent for eacht>0t>0, sincesupx∈D∑k>Ne−t​λk(0)​|vk​φk(0)​(x)|≤e−t​λN+1(0)/2t​‖v‖L2​(D).\sup_{x\in D}\sum_{k>N}e^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}|v_{k}\varphi_{k}^{(0)}(x)|\leq\frac{e^{-t\lambda_{N+1}^{(0)}/2}}{\sqrt{t}}\,\|v\|_{L^{2}(D)}.(4.20)

Consequently,ℰ​(Uμ,Pt(0)​v)\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(U_{\mu},P_{t}^{(0)}v)=∑k≥1ak​(μ)​e−t​λk(0)​vk=∫DPt(0)​v​𝑑μ,\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}a_{k}(\mu)e^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}v_{k}=\int_{D}P_{t}^{(0)}v\,d\mu,‖Pt(0)​v−v‖∞\displaystyle\|P_{t}^{(0)}v-v\|_{\infty}≤t​‖𝖫0​v‖∞,\displaystyle\leq t\|\mathsf{L}_{0}v\|_{\infty},‖Pt(0)​v−v‖ℋ2\displaystyle\|P_{t}^{(0)}v-v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=∑k≥1λk(0)​|e−t​λk(0)−1|2​|vk|2⟶0.\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\lambda_{k}^{(0)}|e^{-t\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}-1|^{2}|v_{k}|^{2}\longrightarrow 0.

Thusℰ​(Uμ,v)=∫Dv​𝑑μ,|∫Dv​𝑑μ|≤ℐD​(μ)1/2​ℰ​(v,v)1/2.\mathcal{E}(U_{\mu},v)=\int_{D}v\,d\mu,\qquad\left|\int_{D}v\,d\mu\right|\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(v,v)^{1/2}.(4.21)

Forv∈Cc∞​(D)v\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D), putFδ​(v)=(v2+δ2)1/2−δF_{\delta}(v)=(v^{2}+\delta^{2})^{1/2}-\delta. ThenFδ​(v)∈Cc∞​(D),Fδ​(v)↑|v|(δ↓0),|∇Fδ​(v)|≤|∇v|,F_{\delta}(v)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D),\qquad F_{\delta}(v)\uparrow|v|\quad(\delta\downarrow 0),\qquad|\nabla F_{\delta}(v)|\leq|\nabla v|,

and hence∫D|v|​𝑑μ≤ℐD​(μ)1/2​ℰ​(v,v)1/2.\int_{D}|v|\,d\mu\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(v,v)^{1/2}.(4.22)

For compactK⊂DK\subset D, withCap1⁡(K):=inf{ℰ​(v,v)+‖v∥L2​(d​x)2:v∈Cc∞​(D),v≥1​near​K},\operatorname{Cap}_{1}(K):=\inf\{\mathcal{E}(v,v)+\|v\|_{L^{2}(dx)}^{2}:v\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D),\ v\geq 1\text{ near }K\},

(4.22) givesμ(K)≤ℐD(μ)1/2Cap1(K)1/2.\mu(K)\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)^{1/2}\operatorname{Cap}_{1}(K)^{1/2}.

The Choquet property ofCap1\operatorname{Cap}_{1}and the inner regularity of the
Radon measureμ\munow imply that, for every exceptional Borel setNN,μ​(N)=supK⊂NK​compactμ​(K)=0.\mu(N)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\subset N\\
K\text{ compact}\end{subarray}}\mu(K)=0.

Thusμ\mucharges no exceptional set and is a smooth finite-energy-integral
measure. We record explicitly how the pairing reaches quasi-continuous
representatives. Givenv∈H01​(D)v\in H_{0}^{1}(D), choosevn∈Cc∞​(D)v_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)withvn→vv_{n}\to vinH01​(D)H_{0}^{1}(D). By (4.22),∫D|vn−vm|​𝑑μ≤ℐD​(μ)1/2​ℰ​(vn−vm,vn−vm)1/2,\int_{D}|v_{n}-v_{m}|\,d\mu\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(v_{n}-v_{m},v_{n}-v_{m})^{1/2},(4.23)

so(vn)(v_{n})is Cauchy inL1​(μ)L^{1}(\mu). After taking a subsequence, the standard
capacity convergence theorem givesvnj→v~v_{n_{j}}\to\widetilde{v}quasi-everywhere;
becauseμ\mucharges no exceptional set, this convergence holdsμ\mu-almost
everywhere. Fatou’s lemma and (4.22)
also give∫D|v~|​𝑑μ≤lim infj→∞∫D|vnj|​𝑑μ≤ℐD​(μ)1/2​ℰ​(v,v)1/2.\int_{D}|\widetilde{v}|\,d\mu\leq\liminf_{j\to\infty}\int_{D}|v_{n_{j}}|\,d\mu\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(v,v)^{1/2}.

TheL1​(μ)L^{1}(\mu)limit in (4.23) is thereforev~\widetilde{v}. Passing to the limit in the core identity givesℰ​(Uμ,v)=limj→∞ℰ​(Uμ,vnj)=limj→∞∫Dvnj​𝑑μ=∫Dv~​𝑑μ,v∈H01​(D).\mathcal{E}(U_{\mu},v)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{E}(U_{\mu},v_{n_{j}})=\lim_{j\to\infty}\int_{D}v_{n_{j}}\,d\mu=\int_{D}\widetilde{v}\,d\mu,\qquad v\in H_{0}^{1}(D).(4.24)

This is precisely the finite-energy-integral extension of[12, Section 2.2, especially Theorem 2.2.5].

For positiveμ1,μ2\mu_{1},\mu_{2}of finite self-energy,ℐD​(μ1,μ2)\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=∑k≥1ak​(μ1)​ak​(μ2)λk(0),\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{a_{k}(\mu_{1})a_{k}(\mu_{2})}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}},(4.25)∑k≥1|ak​(μ1)​ak​(μ2)|λk(0)\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{|a_{k}(\mu_{1})a_{k}(\mu_{2})|}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}≤ℐD​(μ1)1/2​ℐD​(μ2)1/2.\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1})^{1/2}\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{2})^{1/2}.(4.26)

Indeed, uniform absolute convergence gives, for everyε>0\varepsilon>0,∬D2Gε​(x,y)​μ1​(d​x)​μ2​(d​y)=∑k≥1e−ε​λk(0)λk(0)​ak​(μ1)​ak​(μ2).\iint_{D^{2}}G_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\,\mu_{1}(dx)\mu_{2}(dy)=\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{e^{-\varepsilon\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}a_{k}(\mu_{1})a_{k}(\mu_{2}).(4.27)

Cauchy–Schwarz and (4.18) give∑k≥1|ak​(μ1)​ak​(μ2)|λk(0)≤ℐD​(μ1)1/2​ℐD​(μ2)1/2.\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{|a_{k}(\mu_{1})a_{k}(\mu_{2})|}{\lambda_{k}^{(0)}}\leq\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1})^{1/2}\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{2})^{1/2}.

Monotone convergence on the left of
(4.27) and dominated convergence on
the right prove (4.25).
Equations

(4.25) giveℰ​(Uμ1,Uμ2)=ℐD​(μ1,μ2).\mathcal{E}(U_{\mu_{1}},U_{\mu_{2}})=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}).

Apply these identities to the four pairs of Jordan parts and define𝒰D​νj:=Uνj+−Uνj−\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu_{j}:=U_{\nu_{j}^{+}}-U_{\nu_{j}^{-}}. Bilinearity and Hilbert-space

The potential representation makes the signed Green energy positive definite.

## Lemma 4.6.

Ifν∈𝔐ℰ​(D)\nu\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D)andν≠0\nu\neq 0, thenℐD​(ν)>0.\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu)>0.(4.28)

## Proof.

IfℐD​(ν)=0\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu)=0, then
(4.11) gives𝒰D​ν=0\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu=0inℋ\mathcal{H}.
Hence, for everyϕ∈Cc∞​(D)\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D),∫Dϕ​𝑑ν=⟨𝒰D​ν,ϕ⟩ℋ=0.\int_{D}\phi\,d\nu=\langle\mathscr{U}_{D}\nu,\phi\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=0.

Thusν=0\nu=0as a distribution and therefore as a finite signed Radon measure.
∎

## 4.3The Liouville Green operator

The Green inverse and its Hilbert–Schmidt consequences are known in standard
LBM settings[5, Proposition 5.2],[17, Section 3], and[2, Corollary 2.7]. Here we record the rough-domain
Dirichlet-GFF version, its annealed estimate, and a realization measurable in the
canonical GMC field.

## Lemma 4.7.

Almost surely,Mh​(U)>0for every non-empty open​U⊂D.M_{h}(U)>0\qquad\text{for every non-empty open }U\subset D.(4.29)

## Proof.

Work on the full-probability eventΩtr\Omega_{\rm tr}from Section3.
If a non-empty open setU⊂DU\subset DsatisfiedMh​(U)=0M_{h}(U)=0, choose0≠ϕ∈Cc∞​(U)0\neq\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(U). Thenϕ~=0\widetilde{\phi}=0MhM_{h}-almost everywhere.
Full quasi-support would implyϕ=0\phi=0quasi-everywhere and hence inH01​(D)H_{0}^{1}(D),
a contradiction.
∎

Assume0<γ<20<\gamma<2. Almost surely,∬D2GD​(x,y)2​Mh​(d​x)​Mh​(d​y)<∞.\iint_{D^{2}}G_{D}(x,y)^{2}\,M_{h}(dx)M_{h}(dy)<\infty.(4.30)

defines a positive self-adjoint Hilbert–Schmidt operator onL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h}); the

OnΩFr\Omega_{\rm Fr},∬GD​(x,y)2​𝑑Mh​(x)​𝑑Mh​(y)≤Mh​(D)​supx∫GD​(x,y)2​𝑑Mh​(y)<∞\iint G_{D}(x,y)^{2}\,dM_{h}(x)dM_{h}(y)\leq M_{h}(D)\sup_{x}\int G_{D}(x,y)^{2}\,dM_{h}(y)<\infty(4.36)

by Lemmas3.11and3.12. Ifγ2<2\gamma^{2}<2, then𝔼​∬GD2​𝑑Mh​𝑑Mh≤C​∬D2(1+log+⁡|x−y|−1)2​|x−y|−γ2​𝑑x​𝑑y<∞,\mathbb{E}\iint G_{D}^{2}\,dM_{h}\,dM_{h}\leq C\iint_{D^{2}}(1+\log^{+}|x-y|^{-1})^{2}|x-y|^{-\gamma^{2}}\,dx\,dy<\infty,

which is (4.31).

so Fubini yields (4.34) and

give self-adjointness and positivity.

## Proof.

## 5.3Finite-dimensional Gaussian slices

We fix Borel Gaussian coordinates and disintegrate along their finite-dimensional
span, using the measurable-linear-functional construction of[6, Section 2.10]. The spectral series selects
representatives compatible with the fiberwise argument.
Letι:ℋ↪𝖷\iota:\mathcal{H}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{X}be the Cameron–Martin embedding. Forg∈ℋg\in\mathcal{H}, writegk=⟨g,φk(0)⟩L2​(d​x)g_{k}=\langle g,\varphi_{k}^{(0)}\rangle_{L^{2}(dx)}and define the
Borel mapW^g​(x):={limN→∞∑k=1Nλk(0)​gk​⟨x,φk(0)⟩,if the limit exists,0,otherwise.\widehat{W}_{g}(x):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\lambda_{k}^{(0)}g_{k}\langle x,\varphi_{k}^{(0)}\rangle,&\text{if the limit exists},\\[5.69054pt]
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}(5.10)

ThenW​(g):=W^g​(h),𝔼​[W​(f)​W​(g)]=∑k≥1λk(0)​fk​gk=⟨f,g⟩ℋ,W​(ι∗​ℓ)=ℓ​(h).W(g):=\widehat{W}_{g}(h),\qquad\mathbb{E}[W(f)W(g)]=\sum_{k\geq 1}\lambda_{k}^{(0)}f_{k}g_{k}=\langle f,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},\qquad W(\iota^{*}\ell)=\ell(h).(5.11)

ThusW:ℋ→L2​(Ω)W:\mathcal{H}\to L^{2}(\Omega)is the isonormal coordinate field and every
coordinate used below has the fixed Borel representative
(5.10).

## Lemma 5.3.

Letg1,…,gNg_{1},\ldots,g_{N}be a basis ofV⊂ℋV\subset\mathcal{H}, with Gram matrix𝐂V:=(⟨gi,gj⟩ℋ)i,j=1N.\mathbf{C}_{V}:=\bigl(\langle g_{i},g_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\bigr)_{i,j=1}^{N}.(5.12)

Use the representativesW^gi\widehat{W}_{g_{i}}from
(5.10) and define the Borel maps𝐭​(x):=𝐂V−1​(W^g1​(x),…,W^gN​(x))𝖳,v𝐚:=∑j=1Naj​gj,x⟂:=x−v𝐭​(x).\mathbf{t}(x):=\mathbf{C}_{V}^{-1}\bigl(\widehat{W}_{g_{1}}(x),\ldots,\widehat{W}_{g_{N}}(x)\bigr)^{\mathsf{T}},\qquad v_{\mathbf{a}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{N}a_{j}g_{j},\qquad x^{\perp}:=x-v_{\mathbf{t}(x)}.(5.13)

For the canonical fieldhh, the random variables𝐭:=𝐭​(h)\mathbf{t}:=\mathbf{t}(h)andh⟂h^{\perp}are independent, and𝐭\mathbf{t}has the everywhere-positive
densityφV​(𝐚)=(2​π)−N/2​(det𝐂V)1/2​exp⁡(−12​𝐚𝖳​𝐂V​𝐚),𝐚∈ℝN,\varphi_{V}(\mathbf{a})=(2\pi)^{-N/2}(\det\mathbf{C}_{V})^{1/2}\exp\!\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{a}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{C}_{V}\mathbf{a}\right),\qquad\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},(5.14)

and, for every non-negative BorelF:𝖷→[0,∞]F:\mathsf{X}\to[0,\infty],𝔼​[F​(h)]=∫𝖷∫ℝNF​(x⟂+v𝐚)​φV​(𝐚)​𝑑𝐚​ℙV⟂​(d​x⟂),\mathbb{E}[F(h)]=\int_{\mathsf{X}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}F(x^{\perp}+v_{\mathbf{a}})\varphi_{V}(\mathbf{a})\,d\mathbf{a}\,\mathbb{P}_{V}^{\perp}(dx^{\perp}),(5.15)

whereℙV⟂\mathbb{P}_{V}^{\perp}is the law ofh⟂h^{\perp}.

## Proof.

This is the usual finite-dimensional product decomposition of an abstract
Wiener space; see, for example,[6, Sections 2.10 and 3.10]. We include the short
argument to fix the Gram-matrix convention and the Borel representatives needed
by Lemma5.4.
With the Borel representatives in
(5.10),Y:=(W​(g1),…,W​(gN))𝖳∼N​(0,𝐂V),𝐭=𝐂V−1​Y,Cov⁡(𝐭)=𝐂V−1.Y:=\bigl(W(g_{1}),\ldots,W(g_{N})\bigr)^{\mathsf{T}}\sim N(0,\mathbf{C}_{V}),\qquad\mathbf{t}=\mathbf{C}_{V}^{-1}Y,\qquad\operatorname{Cov}(\mathbf{t})=\mathbf{C}_{V}^{-1}.

This is (5.14).

Forℓ∈𝖷∗\ell\in\mathsf{X}^{*}and1≤j≤N1\leq j\leq N,Cov⁡(ℓ​(h),𝐭j)\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}(\ell(h),\mathbf{t}_{j})=∑a=1N(𝐂V−1)j​a​⟨ι∗​ℓ,ga⟩ℋ,\displaystyle=\sum_{a=1}^{N}(\mathbf{C}_{V}^{-1})_{ja}\langle\iota^{*}\ell,g_{a}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},Cov⁡(ℓ​(v𝐭),𝐭j)\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}(\ell(v_{\mathbf{t}}),\mathbf{t}_{j})=∑k=1N⟨ι∗​ℓ,gk⟩ℋ​(𝐂V−1)k​j.\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\langle\iota^{*}\ell,g_{k}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbf{C}_{V}^{-1})_{kj}.

The two expressions agree because𝐂V−1\mathbf{C}_{V}^{-1}is symmetric. HenceCov⁡(ℓ​(h⟂),𝐭j)=0\operatorname{Cov}(\ell(h^{\perp}),\mathbf{t}_{j})=0. Every finite vector formed
from𝖷∗\mathsf{X}^{*}-coordinates ofh⟂h^{\perp}and coordinates of𝐭\mathbf{t}is
Gaussian. Since𝖷\mathsf{X}is separable and its Borel sigma-field is generated by𝖷∗\mathsf{X}^{*}, this zero cross-covariance implies independence. Finally, the map(x⟂,𝐚)↦x⟂+v𝐚(x^{\perp},\mathbf{a})\mapsto x^{\perp}+v_{\mathbf{a}}is Borel, and the product-law
formula gives (5.15).
∎

Building on this factorization, the next lemma produces one coherent
all-parameter GMC family on almost every fiber while retaining canonical
agreement at every good parameter.

## 5.4Holomorphic form perturbation on a slice

On a good coherent fiber, the common-domain calculation applies simultaneously
to all slice parameters.

## 5.5Finite-slice spectral Borel structure

We finally encode the preceding local analytic data into the global Borel
predicates required by Gaussian slicing.

## Lemma 5.6.

Lete1,…,eN∈ℋrege_{1},\ldots,e_{N}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}be linearly independent, and let(Mht)t∈ℝN(M_{h_{t}})_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}be a coherent family withht=h∗+∑k=1Ntk​ek,d​Mht=eγ​∑k=1N(tk−sk)​ek​d​Mhs,s,t∈ℝN.h_{t}=h_{*}+\sum_{k=1}^{N}t_{k}e_{k},\qquad dM_{h_{t}}=e^{\gamma\sum_{k=1}^{N}(t_{k}-s_{k})e_{k}}dM_{h_{s}},\qquad s,t\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.(5.33)

of full quasi-support andAhs0A_{h_{s_{0}}}has compact resolvent. Then these
properties hold for everyt∈ℝNt\in\mathbb{R}^{N}, and every ordered eigenvalue mapt↦Λnhtt\mapsto\Lambda_{n}^{h_{t}}is continuous.

Fixm≥1m\geq 1,1≤n1<⋯<nm1\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{m}, andg1,…,gm∈ℋregg_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}. For eachiiand eachttsuch thatΛniht\Lambda_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}is simple, letϕniht\phi_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}be a normalized
real eigenfunction and, forg∈ℋregg\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}, define the sign-independent functionalℓiht​(g):=−γ​Λniht​∫Dg​(ϕniht)2​𝑑Mht.\ell_{i}^{h_{t}}(g):=-\gamma\Lambda_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}\int_{D}g(\phi_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}})^{2}\,dM_{h_{t}}.(5.34)

Then{t:Λn1ht,…,Λnmht​are simple and​det(ℓiht​(gj))i,j=1m≠0}\left\{t:\ \Lambda_{n_{1}}^{h_{t}},\ldots,\Lambda_{n_{m}}^{h_{t}}\text{ are simple and }\det\bigl(\ell_{i}^{h_{t}}(g_{j})\bigr)_{i,j=1}^{m}\neq 0\right\}(5.35)

is Borel inℝN\mathbb{R}^{N}.

## Proof.

Fixs:=s0s:=s_{0}and writevt,s=∑k=1N(tk−sk)​ek.v_{t,s}=\sum_{k=1}^{N}(t_{k}-s_{k})e_{k}.

The densityeγ​vt,se^{\gamma v_{t,s}}is bounded above and below by positive constants.
Thus finiteness, smoothness, and full quasi-support pass fromMhsM_{h_{s}}toMhtM_{h_{t}}. Full quasi-support implies full topological support by the argument of
Lemma4.7. The disjoint-ball argument in the
proof of Proposition4.11then shows that everyL2​(Mht)L^{2}(M_{h_{t}})is infinite-dimensional. Define the unitary mapUt,s:L2​(Mht)→L2​(Mhs),Ut,s​u=eγ​vt,s/2​u.U_{t,s}:L^{2}(M_{h_{t}})\to L^{2}(M_{h_{s}}),\qquad U_{t,s}u=e^{\gamma v_{t,s}/2}u.

Lemma5.5, withhsh_{s}as base and
then re-centered at each parameter, gives a common form domain, compact
resolvent, and local
norm-resolvent analyticity. Hence every ordered eigenvalue is locally, and
therefore globally, continuous.

PutΛ0ht=0\Lambda_{0}^{h_{t}}=0. For rational vectors𝜶=(αi)\boldsymbol{\alpha}=(\alpha_{i})and𝜷=(βi)\boldsymbol{\beta}=(\beta_{i})with0<αi<βi0<\alpha_{i}<\beta_{i}, define the open set𝒲𝜶,𝜷:=⋂i=1m{Λni−1ht<αi<Λniht<βi<Λni+1ht}.\mathcal{W}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{m}\{\Lambda_{n_{i}-1}^{h_{t}}<\alpha_{i}<\Lambda_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}<\beta_{i}<\Lambda_{n_{i}+1}^{h_{t}}\}.(5.36)

Fort∈𝒲𝜶,𝜷t\in\mathcal{W}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}, letΠiht=𝟏(αi,βi)​(Aht)\Pi_{i}^{h_{t}}=\mathbf{1}_{(\alpha_{i},\beta_{i})}(A_{h_{t}})and transport it to the fixed space:Π^i​(t)=Ut,s​Πiht​Ut,s−1.\widehat{\Pi}_{i}(t)=U_{t,s}\Pi_{i}^{h_{t}}U_{t,s}^{-1}.

It has rank one and is locally real analytic. Ifϕniht\phi_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}is normalized
inL2​(Mht)L^{2}(M_{h_{t}}), thenUt,s​ϕnihtU_{t,s}\phi_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}is normalized inL2​(Mhs)L^{2}(M_{h_{s}}), and multiplication byggcommutes withUt,sU_{t,s}. ThereforeTr⁡(Π^i​(t)​Multg⁡Π^i​(t))\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i}(t)\operatorname{Mult}_{g}\widehat{\Pi}_{i}(t)\bigr)=⟨Multg⁡Ut,s​ϕniht,Ut,s​ϕniht⟩L2​(Mhs)\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{Mult}_{g}U_{t,s}\phi_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}},U_{t,s}\phi_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M_{h_{s}})}=∫Dg​(ϕniht)2​𝑑Mht.\displaystyle=\int_{D}g(\phi_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}})^{2}\,dM_{h_{t}}.(5.37)

Thus, on𝒲𝜶,𝜷\mathcal{W}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}},D𝜶,𝜷​(t):=det(−γ​Λniht​Tr⁡(Π^i​(t)​Multgj⁡Π^i​(t)))i,j=1mD_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}(t):=\det\left(-\gamma\Lambda_{n_{i}}^{h_{t}}\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{i}(t)\operatorname{Mult}_{g_{j}}\widehat{\Pi}_{i}(t)\bigr)\right)_{i,j=1}^{m}

is continuous. The set in (5.35) equals⋃𝜶,𝜷∈ℚm0<αi<βi,1≤i≤m(𝒲𝜶,𝜷∩{D𝜶,𝜷≠0}).\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{Q}^{m}\\
0<\alpha_{i}<\beta_{i},\ 1\leq i\leq m\end{subarray}}\left(\mathcal{W}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}\cap\{D_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}\neq 0\}\right).(5.38)

Indeed, simplicity supplies rational isolating windows, and every point of a window
has simple selected eigenvalues. The union is countable and each term is Borel.
∎

## Part IISpectral non-degeneracy

Part I provides the coherent finite-dimensional fibers, fixed-space analytic
perturbation, and measurable spectral data used below.
Section6proves pathwise splitting and
response transversality; Section7converts them by
Gaussian slicing into almost-sure simplicity and joint eigenvalue densities,
resolving the open problem cited in Section1.

## 6Pathwise spectral response calculus and transversality

For an isolated cluster, analytic perturbation identifies the first derivatives
with the eigenvalues of a finite response compression. Green smoothing makes the
entries of this compression continuous in the Cameron–Martin direction. We then
prove two transversality statements: multiple clusters admit a first-order
splitting direction, and the response measures associated with distinct
eigenvalues are linearly independent.

## 6.1Active response of an isolated spectral cluster

Fix a realization for whichMhM_{h}is finite, smooth, and of full quasi-support andAhA_{h}has compact resolvent. LetΛ\Lambdabe an eigenvalue of multiplicityrr,
regardℰh​(Λ)\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)as a real Hilbert space, and choose a realL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h})-orthonormal basisϕ1,…,ϕr\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{r}.

## Definition 6.1.

The active response family of the cluster atΛ\Lambdais𝒩h​(Λ):={−γ​Λ​ψ2​Mh:ψ∈ℰh​(Λ),‖ψ‖L2​(Mh)=1}.\mathcal{N}_{h}(\Lambda):=\left\{-\gamma\Lambda\psi^{2}M_{h}:\psi\in\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda),\ \|\psi\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})}=1\right\}.(6.1)

This family is compact in total variation: the unit sphere ofℰh​(Λ)\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)is compact, and‖γ​Λ​(ψ2−φ2)​Mh‖TV≤γ​Λ​‖ψ−φ‖L2​(Mh)​‖ψ+φ‖L2​(Mh).\|\gamma\Lambda(\psi^{2}-\varphi^{2})M_{h}\|_{\rm TV}\leq\gamma\Lambda\|\psi-\varphi\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})}\|\psi+\varphi\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})}.

The next identity reduces the form derivative to multiplication on the
eigenspace.

## Lemma 6.2.

For everyf∈ℋreg=Cc∞​(D)f\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}=C_{c}^{\infty}(D)and1≤i,j≤r1\leq i,j\leq r,𝔮˙0,f​(ϕi,ϕj)=−γ​Λ​∫Df​ϕi​ϕj​𝑑Mh.\dot{\mathfrak{q}}_{0,f}(\phi_{i},\phi_{j})=-\gamma\Lambda\int_{D}f\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\,dM_{h}.(6.2)

Multiplication byffpreservesℱh\mathcal{F}_{h}, sof​ϕi,f​ϕj∈ℱhf\phi_{i},f\phi_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{h}. Symmetry ofℰ\mathcal{E}and the weak eigenvalue equation giveℰ​(f​ϕi,ϕj)=ℰ​(ϕj,f​ϕi)=Λ​∫Df​ϕi​ϕj​𝑑Mh,ℰ​(ϕi,f​ϕj)=Λ​∫Df​ϕi​ϕj​𝑑Mh.\mathcal{E}(f\phi_{i},\phi_{j})=\mathcal{E}(\phi_{j},f\phi_{i})=\Lambda\int_{D}f\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\,dM_{h},\qquad\mathcal{E}(\phi_{i},f\phi_{j})=\Lambda\int_{D}f\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\,dM_{h}.

as multisets; this description is independent of the chosen orthonormal basis ofℰh​(Λ)\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda).

## 6.2Green smoothing and finite-energy spectral response

Finite Green energy extends the functionalf↦∫Df​ϕi​ϕj​𝑑Mhf\mapsto\int_{D}f\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\,dM_{h}continuously fromℋreg\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}toℋ\mathcal{H}: forf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}},∫Df​ϕi​ϕj​𝑑Mh=⟨𝒰D​(ϕi​ϕj​Mh),f⟩ℋ,\int_{D}f\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\,dM_{h}=\langle\mathscr{U}_{D}(\phi_{i}\phi_{j}M_{h}),f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},

and the right-hand side is continuous forf∈ℋf\in\mathcal{H}.

Lemma3.12gives|𝒢h​θ​(xn)−𝒢h​θ​(x)|≤‖GD​(xn,⋅)−GD​(x,⋅)‖L2​(Mh)​‖θ‖2⟶0.|\mathscr{G}_{h}\theta(x_{n})-\mathscr{G}_{h}\theta(x)|\leq\|G_{D}(x_{n},\cdot)-G_{D}(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})}\|\theta\|_{2}\longrightarrow 0.

Ifu,v∈Cb​(D)u,v\in C_{b}(D)represent the sameL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h})class andsupp⁡Mh=D\operatorname{supp}M_{h}=D, then, for everyε>0\varepsilon>0,{|u−v|>ε}​is open and has​Mh​-mass​0,\{|u-v|>\varepsilon\}\text{ is open and has }M_{h}\text{-mass }0,

hence it is empty. Thusu=vu=v.

Assume thath∈ΩFr∩Ωoph\in\Omega_{\rm Fr}\cap\Omega_{\rm op}. If(Λ,ϕ)(\Lambda,\phi)is anL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h})-normalized eigenpair ofAhA_{h}, thenϕ=Λ​𝒢h​ϕ\phi=\Lambda\mathscr{G}_{h}\phi(6.14)

Lemma4.9giveℰ​(𝒢h​ϕ,v)=∫Dϕ​v~​𝑑Mh,v∈ℱh.\mathcal{E}(\mathscr{G}_{h}\phi,v)=\int_{D}\phi\widetilde{v}\,dM_{h},\qquad v\in\mathcal{F}_{h}.

The resulting finite-energy pairings extend each cluster compression to all

## Proposition 6.9.

LetΛ\Lambdahave multiplicityrrand letϕ1,…,ϕr\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{r}be an orthonormal basis ofℰh​(Λ)\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda). Assumeϑi​jh:=ϕi​ϕj​Mh∈𝔐ℰ​(D),1≤i,j≤r.\vartheta_{ij}^{h}:=\phi_{i}\phi_{j}M_{h}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D),\qquad 1\leq i,j\leq r.(6.18)

Forf∉Cc∞​(D)f\notin C_{c}^{\infty}(D),
(6.19) denotes only this continuous
extension; no analytic-branch assertion is implied.

## Proof.

Lemma4.5gives∫Df~​𝑑ϑi​jh=⟨𝒰D​ϑi​jh,f⟩ℋ,f∈ℋ.\int_{D}\widetilde{f}\,d\vartheta_{ij}^{h}=\langle\mathscr{U}_{D}\vartheta_{ij}^{h},f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},\qquad f\in\mathcal{H}.

Consequently,‖𝖡fkΛ​(h)−𝖡fΛ​(h)‖HS2\displaystyle\|\mathsf{B}_{f_{k}}^{\Lambda}(h)-\mathsf{B}_{f}^{\Lambda}(h)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}=γ2​Λ2​∑i,j=1r|⟨𝒰D​ϑi​jh,fk−f⟩ℋ|2\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\Lambda^{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}|\langle\mathscr{U}_{D}\vartheta_{ij}^{h},f_{k}-f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|^{2}≤γ2​Λ2​‖fk−f‖ℋ2​∑i,j=1rℐD​(ϑi​jh).\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2}\Lambda^{2}\|f_{k}-f\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}\mathscr{I}_{D}(\vartheta_{ij}^{h}).

Density ofCc∞​(D)C_{c}^{\infty}(D)inℋ\mathcal{H}gives uniqueness, and

response matrix.

## Corollary 6.10.

Assume0<γ<20<\gamma<2. There is a single event of probability one on which the

For a multiple cluster, the complementary fact is that the multiplication
compressions cannot all be scalar.

## Proposition 6.16.

LetMhM_{h}be finite withsupp⁡Mh=D\operatorname{supp}M_{h}=D. LetΛ\Lambdabe an

eigenfunctions with bounded continuous representatives. Then there existsf∈Cc∞​(D)f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)such that(∫Df​u2​𝑑Mh∫Df​u​v​𝑑Mh∫Df​u​v​𝑑Mh∫Df​v2​𝑑Mh)\begin{pmatrix}\int_{D}fu^{2}\,dM_{h}&\int_{D}fuv\,dM_{h}\\
\int_{D}fuv\,dM_{h}&\int_{D}fv^{2}\,dM_{h}\end{pmatrix}(6.34)

is not a scalar matrix.

## Proof.

If the matrix were scalar for everyf∈Cc∞​(D)f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D), then∫Df​u​v​𝑑Mh=0,∫Df​(u2−v2)​𝑑Mh=0(f∈Cc∞​(D)).\int_{D}fuv\,dM_{h}=0,\qquad\int_{D}f(u^{2}-v^{2})\,dM_{h}=0\qquad(f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)).

Hence, as finite signed Radon measures,u​v​Mh=0,(u2−v2)​Mh=0.uvM_{h}=0,\qquad(u^{2}-v^{2})M_{h}=0.

Lemma6.11givesu​v=0uv=0andu2=v2u^{2}=v^{2}onDD, henceu=v=0u=v=0onDD. This contradicts‖u‖L2​(Mh)=‖v‖L2​(Mh)=1\|u\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})}=\|v\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})}=1.
∎

The following finite-dimensional lemma upgrades this pairwise obstruction to a

It is real analytic, has rankdd, and satisfies𝖱α​(0)=prα\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(0)=\operatorname{pr}_{\alpha}. Put𝒴α​(ε):=𝖱α​(ε)​prα+(I−𝖱α​(ε))​(I−prα).\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon):=\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)\operatorname{pr}_{\alpha}+(I-\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon))(I-\operatorname{pr}_{\alpha}).

For sufficiently small|ε||\varepsilon|,‖𝖱α​(ε)−prα‖<1\|\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)-\operatorname{pr}_{\alpha}\|<1, hence𝒴α​(ε)\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)is invertible. Its polar correction𝒰α​(ε):=𝒴α​(ε)​(𝒴α​(ε)∗​𝒴α​(ε))−1/2\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon):=\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)\bigl(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)^{*}\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)\bigr)^{-1/2}

is a real-analytic orthogonal operator satisfying𝒰α​(ε)​prα⁡𝒰α​(ε)∗=𝖱α​(ε)\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)\operatorname{pr}_{\alpha}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)^{*}=\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon). Thereforeℐα​(ε):=𝒰α​(ε)|𝒱α:𝒱α⟶Ran⁡𝖱α​(ε)\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon):=\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)|_{\mathscr{V}_{\alpha}}:\mathscr{V}_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ran}\mathsf{R}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)

is a real-analytic family of isometries withℐα​(0)=I𝒱α\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)=I_{\mathscr{V}_{\alpha}}. The effective
operatorℬαeff​(ε):=ℐα​(ε)∗​(B0+ε​B)​ℐα​(ε)\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon):=\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)^{*}(B_{0}+\varepsilon B)\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)

has precisely the cluster eigenvalues inside|z−α|<2​g|z-\alpha|<2g. Sinceℐα​(ε)∗​ℐα​(ε)=I\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\varepsilon)=IandB0​ℐα​(0)=α​ℐα​(0)B_{0}\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)=\alpha\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0),(ℬαeff)′​(0)\displaystyle(\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{eff}})^{\prime}(0)=ℐα′​(0)∗​B0​ℐα​(0)+ℐα​(0)∗​B​ℐα​(0)+ℐα​(0)∗​B0​ℐα′​(0)\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(0)^{*}B_{0}\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)+\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)^{*}B\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)+\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)^{*}B_{0}\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(0)=C+α​(ℐα′​(0)∗​ℐα​(0)+ℐα​(0)∗​ℐα′​(0))=C.\displaystyle=C+\alpha\bigl(\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(0)^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)+\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(0)^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(0)\bigr)=C.

Thereforepr𝒱α⁡B|𝒱α=cB​I𝒱α(B∈𝔅).\operatorname{pr}_{\mathscr{V}_{\alpha}}B|_{\mathscr{V}_{\alpha}}=c_{B}I_{\mathscr{V}_{\alpha}}\qquad(B\in\mathfrak{B}).

Every two-dimensionalL⊂𝒱αL\subset\mathscr{V}_{\alpha}then has scalar compression for everyB∈𝔅B\in\mathfrak{B}, contrary to the hypothesis. HenceB0B_{0}has simple spectrum.

## Theorem 6.18.

Assume thatMhM_{h}is finite, smooth, and of full quasi-support, thatAhA_{h}has compact

Then there existsf∈Cc∞​(D)f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)such that𝖡fΛ​(h)=−γ​Λ​𝖯Λh​Multf|ℰh​(Λ)\mathsf{B}_{f}^{\Lambda}(h)=-\gamma\Lambda\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{h}\operatorname{Mult}_{f}\big|_{\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)}(6.36)

has simple spectrum. Consequently, therranalytic branches issuing fromΛ\Lambdaalongh+τ​fh+\tau fhave pairwise distinct first derivatives atτ=0\tau=0.

## Proof.

Set𝔅Λh:={𝖯ΛhMultf|ℰh​(Λ):f∈Cc∞(D)}⊂Sym(ℰh(Λ)).\mathfrak{B}_{\Lambda}^{h}:=\{\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{h}\operatorname{Mult}_{f}|_{\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)}:f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)\}\subset\operatorname{Sym}(\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)).

For every two-dimensional subspaceL⊂ℰh​(Λ)L\subset\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda), choose an orthonormal
basisu,vu,vofLL. Proposition6.16suppliesf∈Cc∞​(D)f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)such that the compression of𝖯Λh​Multf|ℰh​(Λ)\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{h}\operatorname{Mult}_{f}|_{\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)}toLLis non-scalar. Hence
Lemma6.17gives anfffor which𝖯Λh​Multf|ℰh​(Λ)\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{h}\operatorname{Mult}_{f}|_{\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)}has simple spectrum. Multiplication by the non-zero
scalar−γ​Λ-\gamma\Lambdapreserves simplicity, and
Proposition6.3identifies its eigenvalues with the
branch derivatives.

Finite-energy continuity then reduces the splitting directions to a fixed
countable dense family.

## Lemma 6.19.

Let𝒬reg⊂Cc∞​(D)∖{0}\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(D)\setminus\{0\}be countable and dense inℋ\mathcal{H}. Assume thatMhM_{h}is finite, smooth, and of full quasi-support, thatAhA_{h}has
compact resolvent, thatsupp⁡Mh=D\operatorname{supp}M_{h}=D, that all eigenfunctions have
bounded continuous representatives, and thatu​v​Mh∈𝔐ℰ​(D)whenever​u,v​belong to the same eigenspace.uvM_{h}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D)\qquad\text{whenever }u,v\text{ belong to the same eigenspace}.

Then, for every multiple eigenvalueΛ\Lambda, there isg∈𝒬regg\in\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}such that𝖡gΛ​(h)\mathsf{B}_{g}^{\Lambda}(h)has simple spectrum.

## Proof.

Fix a multiplicity-rreigenvalueΛ\Lambda. By
Theorem6.18, choosef∈Cc∞​(D)f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(D)such that𝖢fΛ=𝖯Λh​Multf|ℰh​(Λ)\mathsf{C}_{f}^{\Lambda}=\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{h}\operatorname{Mult}_{f}|_{\mathscr{E}_{h}(\Lambda)}has simple spectrum. With an
orthonormal basisϕ1,…,ϕr\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{r}, setdf:=minp<q⁡|λp​(𝖢fΛ)−λq​(𝖢fΛ)|>0.d_{f}:=\min_{p<q}|\lambda_{p}(\mathsf{C}_{f}^{\Lambda})-\lambda_{q}(\mathsf{C}_{f}^{\Lambda})|>0.

## 7Gaussian slicing: simplicity and joint eigenvalue densities

We now combine the pathwise transversality results with finite-dimensional
Gaussian disintegration. Analytic zero-set arguments remove spectral collisions,
while local submersion arguments give joint absolute continuity; the countable
splitting cover turns the pathwise directions into fixed Gaussian slices.

## Lemma 7.1.

Inℳfin+​(D)\mathcal{M}_{\rm fin}^{+}(D)with the weak topology, the following sets are Borel:{μ:supp⁡μ=D},{μ:μ⟂d​x},{μ:ℐD​(μ)<∞}.\{\mu:\operatorname{supp}\mu=D\},\qquad\{\mu:\mu\perp dx\},\qquad\{\mu:\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu)<\infty\}.(7.1)

## Proof.

For every BorelA⊂DA\subset D, the evaluation mapμ↦μ​(A)\mu\mapsto\mu(A)is Borel. Conversely, for witnessesOnO_{n}, setN=lim infnOnN=\liminf_{n}O_{n}. Then|N|≤lim infn|On|=0,∑n≥1μ​(D∖On)≤∑n≥12−n<∞.|N|\leq\liminf_{n}|O_{n}|=0,\qquad\sum_{n\geq 1}\mu(D\setminus O_{n})\leq\sum_{n\geq 1}2^{-n}<\infty.

SinceD∖N=lim supn(D∖On)D\setminus N=\limsup_{n}(D\setminus O_{n}), the first Borel–Cantelli lemma givesμ​(D∖N)=0\mu(D\setminus N)=0. Hence both predicates are Borel.

is Borel, first for rectangle indicators and then by the monotone-class theorem.

Equivalently, for anL2​(Mh0)L^{2}(M_{h_{0}})-orthonormal basisϕ1,…,ϕr\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{r}ofℰh0​(Λ)\mathscr{E}_{h_{0}}(\Lambda), the matrix(∫Dg​ϕi​ϕj​𝑑Mh0)1≤i,j≤r\left(\int_{D}g\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\,dM_{h_{0}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}(7.11)

has simple spectrum. This property is independent of the chosen orthonormal basis.

Then there exists𝐠=(g1,…,gm)∈(𝒬den)m\mathbf{g}=(g_{1},\ldots,g_{m})\in(\mathscr{Q}_{\rm den})^{m}such thatdet(ℓih​(gj))1≤i,j≤m≠0.\det\bigl(\ell_{i}^{h}(g_{j})\bigr)_{1\leq i,j\leq m}\neq 0.(7.54)

By Lemma4.6and
(7.53), the Gram matrix(⟨𝒰D​ηnih,𝒰D​ηnjh⟩ℋ)i,j\bigl(\langle\mathscr{U}_{D}\eta_{n_{i}}^{h},\mathscr{U}_{D}\eta_{n_{j}}^{h}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\bigr)_{i,j}is

functionalsℓ1h,…,ℓmh\ell_{1}^{h},\ldots,\ell_{m}^{h}are linearly independent, and the mapℒhspec:ℋ→ℝm,ℒhspec​(f)=(ℓ1h​(f),…,ℓmh​(f))\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\rm spec}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{R}^{m},\qquad\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\rm spec}(f)=(\ell_{1}^{h}(f),\ldots,\ell_{m}^{h}(f))(7.55)

is surjective. Choosefj∈ℋf_{j}\in\mathcal{H}withℒhspec​(fj)=𝐞j\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\rm spec}(f_{j})=\mathbf{e}_{j}, thejjth standard basis vector ofℝm\mathbb{R}^{m}. Thendet(ℓih​(fj))=1\det(\ell_{i}^{h}(f_{j}))=1. By continuity of the determinant and density of(𝒬den)m(\mathscr{Q}_{\rm den})^{m}inℋm\mathcal{H}^{m}, thefjf_{j}may be approximated bygj∈𝒬deng_{j}\in\mathscr{Q}_{\rm den}while preserving a non-zero determinant.

We can now choose a full-rank response tuple from a fixed countable family and

Then(Λn1h,…,Λnmh)#​ℙ≪ℒm.(\Lambda_{n_{1}}^{h},\ldots,\Lambda_{n_{m}}^{h})_{\#}\mathbb{P}\ll\mathcal{L}^{m}.(7.57)

\det(\ell_{i}^{h}(g_{j}))_{i,j=1}^{m}\neq 0\end{array}\right\}.(7.58)

This event is Borel. Indeed, putΛ0h=0\Lambda_{0}^{h}=0. On rational isolating windows
satisfyingΛni−1h<αi<Λnih<βi<Λni+1h,i=1,…,m,\Lambda_{n_{i}-1}^{h}<\alpha_{i}<\Lambda_{n_{i}}^{h}<\beta_{i}<\Lambda_{n_{i}+1}^{h},\qquad i=1,\ldots,m,

Lemma5.3,h=h⟂+vt,d​ℙ​(h)=φV​(t)​d​t​d​ℙV⟂​(h⟂),φV​(t)>0.h=h^{\perp}+v_{t},\qquad d\mathbb{P}(h)=\varphi_{V}(t)\,dt\,d\mathbb{P}_{V}^{\perp}(h^{\perp}),\qquad\varphi_{V}(t)>0.(7.61)

For the canonical data define𝒵h⟂can:={t:h⟂+vt∈Ω𝐧,𝐠sub,(Λn1h⟂+vt,…,Λnmh⟂+vt)∈B}.\mathcal{Z}_{h^{\perp}}^{\rm can}:=\left\{t:\begin{array}[]{l}h^{\perp}+v_{t}\in\Omega_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{g}}^{\rm sub},\\

and agreeing with the canonical family fort∈𝔄0​(h⟂)t\in\mathfrak{A}_{0}(h^{\perp}). LetΛ~n​(t)\widetilde{\Lambda}_{n}(t)be the coherent ordered eigenvalues and let𝒵h⟂coh\mathcal{Z}_{h^{\perp}}^{\rm coh}be

## Corollary 7.9.

Assume0<γ<20<\gamma<2. For everym≥1m\geq 1and1≤n1<⋯<nm1\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{m},(Λn1h,…,Λnmh)#​ℙ≪ℒm.(\Lambda_{n_{1}}^{h},\ldots,\Lambda_{n_{m}}^{h})_{\#}\mathbb{P}\ll\mathcal{L}^{m}.(7.70)

## Proof.

By Theorem7.6, the selected eigenvalues are
pairwise distinct and simple almost surely. OnΩstr∩Ωsimp\Omega_{\rm str}\cap\Omega_{\rm simp},
Lemma6.7supplies
bounded continuous representatives, and
Theorem6.14verifies
the transversality hypothesis
(7.56).
Theorem7.8therefore yields
(7.70).
∎

## 7.3Final proof of Theorem1.1

The full-probability assertion in
Theorem5.1, together with
(7.49), givesℙ​(Ωop∩Ωcmp∩Ωsimp)=1.\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\rm op}\cap\Omega_{\rm cmp}\cap\Omega_{\rm simp})=1.

Forh∈Ωop∩Ωcmp∩Ωsimph\in\Omega_{\rm op}\cap\Omega_{\rm cmp}\cap\Omega_{\rm simp},Ah−1=𝒢h,‖𝒢h‖HS<∞,Rλh=(Ah+λ)−1=(I+λ​𝒢h)−1​𝒢h.A_{h}^{-1}=\mathscr{G}_{h},\qquad\|\mathscr{G}_{h}\|_{\rm HS}<\infty,\qquad R_{\lambda}^{h}=(A_{h}+\lambda)^{-1}=(I+\lambda\mathscr{G}_{h})^{-1}\mathscr{G}_{h}.

ThusRλhR_{\lambda}^{h}is compact, soAhA_{h}has compact resolvent. Ifκ1​(h)≥κ2​(h)≥⋯>0\kappa_{1}(h)\geq\kappa_{2}(h)\geq\cdots>0are the eigenvalues of𝒢h\mathscr{G}_{h}, thenκn​(h)↓0,Λnh=κn​(h)−1,\kappa_{n}(h)\downarrow 0,\qquad\Lambda_{n}^{h}=\kappa_{n}(h)^{-1},

which gives (5.2). Sinceh∈Ωsimph\in\Omega_{\rm simp},
(7.29) makes every inequality
strict. Hence0<Λ1h<Λ2h<⋯↑∞,0<\Lambda_{1}^{h}<\Lambda_{2}^{h}<\cdots\uparrow\infty,

which is (1.3).

Finally, for everym≥1m\geq 1and1≤n1<⋯<nm1\leq n_{1}<\cdots<n_{m},
(7.70) reads(Λn1h,…,Λnmh)#​ℙ≪ℒm,(\Lambda_{n_{1}}^{h},\ldots,\Lambda_{n_{m}}^{h})_{\#}\mathbb{P}\ll\mathcal{L}^{m},

which is exactly (1.4). This proves
Theorem1.1.

## Corollary 7.10.

LetAhLQGA_{h}^{\rm LQG}be the trace-form operator onL2​(MhLQG)L^{2}(M_{h}^{\rm LQG}).
Almost surely,AhLQGA_{h}^{\rm LQG}has simple spectrum, and every finite subvector of
its ordered eigenvalues has a density with respect to Lebesgue measure.

## Proof.

Recall thatMh=MhcanM_{h}=M_{h}^{\rm can}. SetwD=𝔯Dγ2/2.w_{D}=\mathfrak{r}_{D}^{\gamma^{2}/2}.

ThenwD∈C​(D;(0,∞))∩L∞​(D)w_{D}\in C(D;(0,\infty))\cap L^{\infty}(D), and
(3.27) allows us, after modification on a null
event, to use the Borel versionMhLQG​(d​z):=wD​(z)​Mh​(d​z),h∈𝖷.M_{h}^{\rm LQG}(dz):=w_{D}(z)M_{h}(dz),\qquad h\in\mathsf{X}.

## Part IIIResolvent response, Ward identities, and Gaussian densities

The spectral calculus of Part II, combined with Part I’s measure response,
identifies a response formulation of the LQG quantum unique ergodicity problem.
On the simple-spectrum event, setνnh=(ϕnh)2​Mh,M¯h=MhMh​(D).\nu_{n}^{h}=(\phi_{n}^{h})^{2}M_{h},\qquad\overline{M}_{h}=\frac{M_{h}}{M_{h}(D)}.

Then, forf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}},∂flog⁡(Mh​(D)​Λnh)=γ​(∫Df​𝑑M¯h−∫Df​𝑑νnh).\partial_{f}\log\!\bigl(M_{h}(D)\Lambda_{n}^{h}\bigr)=\gamma\left(\int_{D}f\,d\overline{M}_{h}-\int_{D}f\,d\nu_{n}^{h}\right).

Thus the quantum unique ergodicity conjecture[2, Problem 4.15]predicts the vanishing of the
area-normalized high-energy spectral response. Parts I–II supply this pathwise
identity, but not the required high-energy control. A prerequisite for such a
program is a response theory for resolvent- and Laplace-smoothed spectral
observables which does not require following individual eigenbranches; this is
the operator-level calculus developed in Part III. The further high-energy and
de-regularization estimates needed for quantum unique ergodicity are beyond the
scope of this paper.

## 8Resolvent response

Using the coherent measure family and trace-form resolvent of Part I, we derive
a fixed-energy response formula that does not require spectral simplicity.

Fixf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}},h∈Ωcohh\in\Omega_{\rm coh}, and writeμ=Mh,μτ=Mh,τf,coh=eτ​μ,eτ=eγ​τ​f.\mu=M_{h},\qquad\mu_{\tau}=M_{h,\tau}^{f,\mathrm{coh}}=e_{\tau}\mu,\qquad e_{\tau}=e^{\gamma\tau f}.(8.1)

Thenμτ​(D)<∞,μτ∼μ,q​-​suppℰ⁡μτ=D.\mu_{\tau}(D)<\infty,\qquad\mu_{\tau}\sim\mu,\qquad\operatorname{q\text{-}supp}_{\mathcal{E}}\mu_{\tau}=D.

Sinceffis bounded,e−γ​|τ|​‖f‖∞≤eτ≤eγ​|τ|​‖f‖∞;e^{-\gamma|\tau|\|f\|_{\infty}}\leq e_{\tau}\leq e^{\gamma|\tau|\|f\|_{\infty}};

henceL2​(D,μτ)=L2​(D,μ)L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})=L^{2}(D,\mu)as sets with equivalent norms, and thereforeℱμτ=ℱμ\mathcal{F}_{\mu_{\tau}}=\mathcal{F}_{\mu}. Throughout this section,Rλh:=RλμR_{\lambda}^{h}:=R_{\lambda}^{\mu}andRλh+τ​f:=RλμτR_{\lambda}^{h+\tau f}:=R_{\lambda}^{\mu_{\tau}}.

## Lemma 8.1.

Letp∈[1,∞)p\in[1,\infty)andT>0T>0. andδτ⟶γ​fin​Lp​(D,μ),τ→0.\delta_{\tau}\longrightarrow\gamma f\quad\text{in }L^{p}(D,\mu),\qquad\tau\to 0.(8.4)

andeτ→1e_{\tau}\to 1μ\mu-almost everywhere. Lemma3.7and dominated convergence prove (8.3). Alsoδτ=γ​f​∫01es​γ​τ​f​𝑑s,|δτ|p≤γp​|f|p​ep​γ​T​|f|.\delta_{\tau}=\gamma f\int_{0}^{1}e^{s\gamma\tau f}\,ds,\qquad|\delta_{\tau}|^{p}\leq\gamma^{p}|f|^{p}e^{p\gamma T|f|}.

## 9Resolvent Ward identities and causal Laplace representatives

Proposition8.4supplies all differentiability used
below. We apply it to tested operator pairings and their endpoint measures, and
then identify causal Laplace-transform representatives.

Fixf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}},h∈Ωcohh\in\Omega_{\rm coh},λ>0\lambda>0, and setμ=Mh,μτ=Mh,τf,coh=eτ​μ,eτ=eγ​τ​f,Rτ=Rλh+τ​f,R=Rλh.\mu=M_{h},\qquad\mu_{\tau}=M_{h,\tau}^{f,\mathrm{coh}}=e_{\tau}\mu,\qquad e_{\tau}=e^{\gamma\tau f},\qquad R_{\tau}=R_{\lambda}^{h+\tau f},\qquad R=R_{\lambda}^{h}.(9.1)
