## 9.1Operator pairings and the tested kernel convention

## Lemma 9.1.

Letν\nube a finite positive smooth Radon measure of full quasi-support, and letAνA_{\nu}be the non-negative self-adjoint operator associated with(ℰ,ℱν)(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}_{\nu}). Forλ>0\lambda>0andt≥0t\geq 0, putPtν=e−t​Aν,Rλν=(Aν+λ)−1.P_{t}^{\nu}=e^{-tA_{\nu}},\qquad R_{\lambda}^{\nu}=(A_{\nu}+\lambda)^{-1}.

Forg1,g2∈L2​(D,ν)g_{1},g_{2}\in L^{2}(D,\nu),∫Dg1​Rλν​g2​𝑑ν\displaystyle\int_{D}g_{1}R_{\lambda}^{\nu}g_{2}\,d\nu=∫Dg2​Rλν​g1​𝑑ν,\displaystyle=\int_{D}g_{2}R_{\lambda}^{\nu}g_{1}\,d\nu,(9.2)|∫Dg1​Rλν​g2​𝑑ν|\displaystyle\left|\int_{D}g_{1}R_{\lambda}^{\nu}g_{2}\,d\nu\right|≤λ−1​‖g1‖L2​(D,ν)​‖g2‖L2​(D,ν).\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-1}\|g_{1}\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}\|g_{2}\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}.(9.3)

For everyt≥0t\geq 0,PtνP_{t}^{\nu}is self-adjoint and sub-Markovian, with‖Ptν​g‖L2​(D,ν)≤‖g‖L2​(D,ν),g∈L2​(D,ν),\|P_{t}^{\nu}g\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}\leq\|g\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)},\qquad g\in L^{2}(D,\nu),

andRλν​g=∫0∞e−λ​t​Ptν​g​𝑑tin​L2​(D,ν).R_{\lambda}^{\nu}g=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}P_{t}^{\nu}g\,dt\quad\text{in }L^{2}(D,\nu).(9.4)

In particular,0≤Ptν​𝟏≤𝟏,0≤Rλν​𝟏≤λ−1​𝟏ν​-almost everywhere,0\leq P_{t}^{\nu}\mathbf{1}\leq\mathbf{1},\qquad 0\leq R_{\lambda}^{\nu}\mathbf{1}\leq\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{1}\quad\nu\text{-almost everywhere},(9.5)

and‖Ptν​g‖L∞​(D,ν)≤‖g‖L∞​(D,ν),g∈L2​(D,ν)∩L∞​(D,ν).\|P_{t}^{\nu}g\|_{L^{\infty}(D,\nu)}\leq\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(D,\nu)},\qquad g\in L^{2}(D,\nu)\cap L^{\infty}(D,\nu).(9.6)

## Proof.

The resolvent is self-adjoint by
Lemma4.1, and the spectral theorem gives‖Rλν‖2→2≤λ−1\|R_{\lambda}^{\nu}\|_{2\to 2}\leq\lambda^{-1}; this proves
(9.2)–
(9.3).

For every normal contractionC:ℝ→ℝC:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}withC​(0)=0C(0)=0, the
Sobolev chain rule givesC​(u)∈ℱν,ℰ​(C​(u),C​(u))≤ℰ​(u,u),‖C​(u)‖L2​(D,ν)≤‖u‖L2​(D,ν).C(u)\in\mathcal{F}_{\nu},\qquad\mathcal{E}(C(u),C(u))\leq\mathcal{E}(u,u),\qquad\|C(u)\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}\leq\|u\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}.

Thus(ℰ,ℱν)(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}_{\nu})is a symmetric Dirichlet form, so its semigroup is
self-adjoint and sub-Markovian. Hence0≤Ptν​𝟏≤𝟏0\leq P_{t}^{\nu}\mathbf{1}\leq\mathbf{1}and
(9.6) holds. If(𝖤ν​(ξ))ξ≥0(\mathsf{E}_{\nu}(\xi))_{\xi\geq 0}is the spectral resolution ofAνA_{\nu}, then‖Ptν​g‖L2​(D,ν)2=∫[0,∞)e−2​t​ξ​d​‖𝖤ν​(ξ)​g‖L2​(D,ν)2≤‖g‖L2​(D,ν)2.\|P_{t}^{\nu}g\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}^{2}=\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{-2t\xi}\,d\|\mathsf{E}_{\nu}(\xi)g\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}^{2}\leq\|g\|_{L^{2}(D,\nu)}^{2}.

The same spectral resolution gives∫0∞e−λ​t​Ptν​g​𝑑t=∫[0,∞)(∫0∞e−(λ+ξ)​t​𝑑t)​𝑑𝖤ν​(ξ)​g=∫[0,∞)1λ+ξ​𝑑𝖤ν​(ξ)​g,\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}P_{t}^{\nu}g\,dt=\int_{[0,\infty)}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-(\lambda+\xi)t}\,dt\right)d\mathsf{E}_{\nu}(\xi)g=\int_{[0,\infty)}\frac{1}{\lambda+\xi}\,d\mathsf{E}_{\nu}(\xi)g,

which is (9.4). The first assertion in
(9.5) was established above; the second follows from
Lemmas4.4and4.3withψ=𝟏\psi=\mathbf{1}.
∎

Fora,b∈ℬb​(D)a,b\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D), the mapτ↦ℬλ,τ​(a,b)\tau\mapsto\mathcal{B}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)is differentiable at0, anddd​τ|τ=0​ℬλ,τ​(a,b)=γ​∫Df​b​R​a​𝑑μ−γ​λ​∫Df​R​a​R​b​𝑑μ.\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{B}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=\gamma\int_{D}fb\,Ra\,d\mu-\gamma\lambda\int_{D}f\,Ra\,Rb\,d\mu.(9.18)

## Theorem 9.4.

Fora,b∈ℬb​(D)a,b\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D), the mapτ↦𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)\tau\mapsto\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)is differentiable at0, anddd​τ|τ=0​𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)=γ​∫Df​(a​R​b+b​R​a−λ​R​a​R​b)​𝑑μ.\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=\gamma\int_{D}f\bigl(aRb+bRa-\lambda Ra\,Rb\bigr)\,d\mu.(9.22)

This is only a tested bilinear identity; it asserts neither a coherent kernel
choice inτ\taunor total-variation differentiability of the corresponding
two-point measures.

## Proof.

Withuτ=Rτ​bu_{\tau}=R_{\tau}b,u=R​bu=Rb, andδτ=(eτ−1)/τ\delta_{\tau}=(e_{\tau}-1)/\tau,𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)−𝒬λ,0​(a,b)τ=∫Da​uτ−uτ​𝑑μ+∫Da​uτ​δτ​𝑑μ.\frac{\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)-\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(a,b)}{\tau}=\int_{D}a\frac{u_{\tau}-u}{\tau}\,d\mu+\int_{D}au_{\tau}\delta_{\tau}\,d\mu.(9.25)

By Proposition9.3, the first term converges toγ​∫Df​b​R​a​𝑑μ−γ​λ​∫Df​R​a​R​b​𝑑μ.\gamma\int_{D}fb\,Ra\,d\mu-\gamma\lambda\int_{D}f\,Ra\,Rb\,d\mu.

For the second term,∫Da​uτ​δτ​𝑑μ−γ​∫Da​f​R​b​𝑑μ=∫Da​(uτ−u)​δτ​𝑑μ+∫Da​u​(δτ−γ​f)​𝑑μ.\int_{D}au_{\tau}\delta_{\tau}\,d\mu-\gamma\int_{D}afRb\,d\mu=\int_{D}a(u_{\tau}-u)\delta_{\tau}\,d\mu+\int_{D}au(\delta_{\tau}-\gamma f)\,d\mu.

The two errors satisfy|∫Da​(uτ−u)​δτ​𝑑μ|\displaystyle\left|\int_{D}a(u_{\tau}-u)\delta_{\tau}\,d\mu\right|≤‖a‖∞​‖uτ−u‖L2​(D,μ)​‖δτ‖L2​(D,μ)⟶0,\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{\infty}\|u_{\tau}-u\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}\|\delta_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}\longrightarrow 0,|∫Da​u​(δτ−γ​f)​𝑑μ|\displaystyle\left|\int_{D}au(\delta_{\tau}-\gamma f)\,d\mu\right|≤‖a‖∞​‖u‖∞​‖δτ−γ​f‖L1​(D,μ)⟶0,\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{\infty}\|u\|_{\infty}\|\delta_{\tau}-\gamma f\|_{L^{1}(D,\mu)}\longrightarrow 0,

by Proposition8.3,
Lemma8.1, and
Lemma4.3. Substitution into
(9.25) proves (9.22).
The two endpoint terms in (9.23) represent∫Df​a​R​b​𝑑μ\int_{D}faRb\,d\muand∫Df​b​R​a​𝑑μ\int_{D}fbRa\,d\mu, respectively, by symmetry ofrλhr_{\lambda}^{h}. The absolute-integrability estimates in the preceding proof
handle the convolution term and show thatr˙λ,fmov∈L1​(μ⊗μ)\dot{r}_{\lambda,f}^{\rm mov}\in L^{1}(\mu\otimes\mu). Hence

## Lemma 9.6.

PutCa=−γ​f​aC_{a}=-\gamma faandCb=−γ​f​bC_{b}=-\gamma fb. Asτ→0\tau\to 0,𝒬λ,τ​(Ca,b)\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(C_{a},b)⟶𝒬λ,0​(Ca,b),\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{a},b),𝒬λ,τ​(Cb,a)\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(C_{b},a)⟶𝒬λ,0​(Cb,a),\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{b},a),(9.33)𝒬λ,τ​(a​ζτ,b​sτ)\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a\zeta_{\tau},bs_{\tau})⟶𝒬λ,0​(Ca,b),\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{a},b),𝒬λ,τ​(a,b​ζτ)\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b\zeta_{\tau})⟶𝒬λ,0​(a,Cb).\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(a,C_{b}).(9.34)

## Proof.

We prove the first limit in (9.33); the second follows by
interchangingaaandbb. Sinced​μτ=eτ​d​μd\mu_{\tau}=e_{\tau}d\mu,𝒬λ,τ​(Ca,b)−𝒬λ,0​(Ca,b)=∫DCa​(Rτ​b−R​b)​eτ​𝑑μ+∫DCa​R​b​(eτ−1)​𝑑μ.\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(C_{a},b)-\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{a},b)=\int_{D}C_{a}(R_{\tau}b-Rb)e_{\tau}\,d\mu+\int_{D}C_{a}Rb(e_{\tau}-1)\,d\mu.

For|τ|≤T|\tau|\leq T,‖Ca​eτ‖L2​(D,μ)2≤γ2​‖a‖∞2​∫D|f|2​e2​γ​T​|f|​𝑑μ<∞.\|C_{a}e_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{D}|f|^{2}e^{2\gamma T|f|}\,d\mu<\infty.

Hence the first term tends to zero by
Proposition8.3. The second tends to zero by
dominated convergence, because|Ca​R​b​(eτ−1)|≤γ​‖a‖∞​‖R​b‖∞​|f|​(eγ​T​|f|+1)∈L1​(D,μ).|C_{a}Rb(e_{\tau}-1)|\leq\gamma\|a\|_{\infty}\|Rb\|_{\infty}|f|\bigl(e^{\gamma T|f|}+1\bigr)\in L^{1}(D,\mu).

For the first limit in (9.34), bilinearity gives𝒬λ,τ​(a​ζτ,b​sτ)−𝒬λ,0​(Ca,b)\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a\zeta_{\tau},bs_{\tau})-\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{a},b)=𝒬λ,τ​(a​ζτ−Ca,b​sτ)+𝒬λ,τ​(Ca,b​(sτ−1))+𝒬λ,τ​(Ca,b)−𝒬λ,0​(Ca,b).\displaystyle\quad=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a\zeta_{\tau}-C_{a},bs_{\tau})+\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(C_{a},b(s_{\tau}-1))+\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(C_{a},b)-\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{a},b).

By (9.15), the first two terms are bounded respectively byλ−1​‖a​(ζτ+γ​f)‖L2​(D,μτ)​‖b​sτ‖L2​(D,μτ),\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\|a(\zeta_{\tau}+\gamma f)\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})}\|bs_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})},λ−1​‖Ca‖L2​(D,μτ)​‖b​(sτ−1)‖L2​(D,μτ).\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\|C_{a}\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})}\|b(s_{\tau}-1)\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})}.

They tend to zero by Lemma9.5; the
remaining fixed factors are uniformly bounded, withsup|τ|≤T‖Ca‖L2​(D,μτ)2≤γ2​‖a‖∞2​∫D|f|2​eγ​T​|f|​𝑑μ<∞.\sup_{|\tau|\leq T}\|C_{a}\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})}^{2}\leq\gamma^{2}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{D}|f|^{2}e^{\gamma T|f|}\,d\mu<\infty.

The fixed-pairing limit proves the first assertion in
(9.34). For the second, self-adjointness gives𝒬λ,τ​(a,b​ζτ)=𝒬λ,τ​(b​ζτ,a),𝒬λ,0​(Cb,a)=𝒬λ,0​(a,Cb).\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b\zeta_{\tau})=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(b\zeta_{\tau},a),\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{b},a)=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(a,C_{b}).

Furthermore,|𝒬λ,τ​(b​ζτ,a)−𝒬λ,0​(Cb,a)|\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(b\zeta_{\tau},a)-\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{b},a)\right|≤λ−1​‖b​(ζτ+γ​f)‖L2​(D,μτ)​‖a‖L2​(D,μτ)+|𝒬λ,τ​(Cb,a)−𝒬λ,0​(Cb,a)|.\displaystyle\quad\leq\lambda^{-1}\|b(\zeta_{\tau}+\gamma f)\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})}\|a\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu_{\tau})}+\left|\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(C_{b},a)-\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(C_{b},a)\right|.

The first term tends to zero by
Lemma9.5; the second tends to zero by
(9.33). The limit is therefore𝒬λ,0​(a,Cb)\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(a,C_{b}), as required.

Fora,b∈ℬb​(D)a,b\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D), the mapτ↦𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)\tau\mapsto\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)is differentiable at0, anddd​τ|τ=0​𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)=−γ​λ​∫Df​R​a​R​b​𝑑μ.\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=-\gamma\lambda\int_{D}f\,Ra\,Rb\,d\mu.(9.35)

Ifh∈Ωcoh∩Ωhkh\in\Omega_{\rm coh}\cap\Omega_{\rm hk}, definer˙λ,ffb​(x,y):=−γ​λ​∫Drλh​(x,z)​f​(z)​rλh​(z,y)​μ​(d​z).\dot{r}_{\lambda,f}^{\rm fb}(x,y):=-\gamma\lambda\int_{D}r_{\lambda}^{h}(x,z)f(z)r_{\lambda}^{h}(z,y)\,\mu(dz).(9.36)

Thenr˙λ,ffb∈L1​(D2,μ⊗μ)\dot{r}_{\lambda,f}^{\rm fb}\in L^{1}(D^{2},\mu\otimes\mu)anddd​τ|τ=0​𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)=∬D2a​(x)​r˙λ,ffb​(x,y)​b​(y)​μ​(d​x)​μ​(d​y).\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)=\iint_{D^{2}}a(x)\dot{r}_{\lambda,f}^{\rm fb}(x,y)b(y)\,\mu(dx)\mu(dy).(9.37)

Thus (9.37) is the weak meaning of∂frλh=r˙λ,ffb\partial_{f}r_{\lambda}^{h}=\dot{r}_{\lambda,f}^{\rm fb}.

## Proof.

WriteA=aA=a,B=bB=b,Aτ=a​sτA_{\tau}=as_{\tau}, andBτ=b​sτB_{\tau}=bs_{\tau}. Then𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)−𝒥λ,0​(a,b)\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)-\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,0}(a,b)=𝒬λ,τ​(A,B)−𝒬λ,0​(A,B)\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(A,B)-\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,0}(A,B)+𝒬λ,τ​(Aτ−A,Bτ)+𝒬λ,τ​(A,Bτ−B).\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(A_{\tau}-A,B_{\tau})+\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(A,B_{\tau}-B).

After division byτ\tau, Theorem9.4gives the
limit of the first term,γ​∫Df​a​R​b​𝑑μ+γ​∫Df​b​R​a​𝑑μ−γ​λ​∫Df​R​a​R​b​𝑑μ.\gamma\int_{D}faRb\,d\mu+\gamma\int_{D}fbRa\,d\mu-\gamma\lambda\int_{D}fRaRb\,d\mu.

Lemma9.6gives the remaining limits𝒬λ,τ​(a​ζτ,b​sτ)⟶−γ​∫Df​a​R​b​𝑑μ,𝒬λ,τ​(a,b​ζτ)⟶−γ​∫Df​b​R​a​𝑑μ.\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a\zeta_{\tau},bs_{\tau})\longrightarrow-\gamma\int_{D}faRb\,d\mu,\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b\zeta_{\tau})\longrightarrow-\gamma\int_{D}fbRa\,d\mu.

The endpoint terms cancel, proving
(9.35). The absolute-integrability estimate
in the proof of Proposition9.3shows thatr˙λ,ffb∈L1​(μ⊗μ)\dot{r}_{\lambda,f}^{\rm fb}\in L^{1}(\mu\otimes\mu)and identifies the

## 9.4Causal Laplace-transform representatives of the Ward responses

Fort≥0t\geq 0, setℋt,τ​(a,b)=∫Da​Pth+τ​f​b​𝑑μτ.\mathcal{H}_{t,\tau}(a,b)=\int_{D}aP_{t}^{h+\tau f}b\,d\mu_{\tau}.(9.38)

Strong continuity and theL2L^{2}contraction give|ℋt,τ​(a,b)|≤‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞​μτ​(D),∫0∞e−λ​t​|ℋt,τ​(a,b)|​𝑑t<∞,|\mathcal{H}_{t,\tau}(a,b)|\leq\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}\mu_{\tau}(D),\qquad\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}|\mathcal{H}_{t,\tau}(a,b)|\,dt<\infty,

and∫0∞e−λ​t​ℋt,τ​(a,b)​𝑑t=𝒬λ,τ​(a,b).\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\mathcal{H}_{t,\tau}(a,b)\,dt=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b).(9.39)

For comparison, define the fixed-base heat pairingℋt,τfb​(a,b):=∫Da​sτ​Pth+τ​f​(b​sτ)​𝑑μτ.\mathcal{H}_{t,\tau}^{\rm fb}(a,b):=\int_{D}as_{\tau}\,P_{t}^{h+\tau f}(bs_{\tau})\,d\mu_{\tau}.(9.40)

The inverse-multiplier bounds and theL2​(μτ)L^{2}(\mu_{\tau})contraction make this
pairing well defined, and the semigroup–resolvent identity gives∫0∞e−λ​t​ℋt,τfb​(a,b)​𝑑t=𝒥λ,τ​(a,b).\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\mathcal{H}_{t,\tau}^{\rm fb}(a,b)\,dt=\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b).(9.41)

These pairings identify the observables behind the two Laplace transforms.

Let𝒮+′​(ℝ)={T∈𝒮′​(ℝ):supp⁡T⊂[0,∞)}.\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathbb{R})=\{T\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}):\operatorname{supp}T\subset[0,\infty)\}.

Chooseχ∈C∞​(ℝ)\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})withχ=0\chi=0on(−∞,−1](-\infty,-1]andχ=1\chi=1on[−1/2,∞)[-1/2,\infty). ForT∈𝒮+′​(ℝ)T\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathbb{R}), defineℒ​[T]​(λ)=⟨T,χ​(t)​e−λ​t⟩,λ>0.\mathscr{L}[T](\lambda)=\langle T,\chi(t)e^{-\lambda t}\rangle,\qquad\lambda>0.(9.42)

This is independent ofχ\chi. We use the standard injectivity of the Laplace
transform on𝒮+′​(ℝ)\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathbb{R})only for uniqueness of the causal
representatives below. Moreover,ℒ​[∂tT]​(λ)=λ​ℒ​[T]​(λ),\mathscr{L}[\partial_{t}T](\lambda)=\lambda\mathscr{L}[T](\lambda),(9.43)

becausesupp⁡χ′∩[0,∞)=∅\operatorname{supp}\chi^{\prime}\cap[0,\infty)=\varnothing. For locally
integrableggof polynomial growth,⟨Ext+⁡g,φ⟩=∫0∞g​(t)​φ​(t)​𝑑t,φ∈𝒮​(ℝ).\langle\operatorname{Ext}_{+}g,\varphi\rangle=\int_{0}^{\infty}g(t)\varphi(t)\,dt,\qquad\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}).

Defineℭth​(a,b;f)=∫0t∫Df​Pt−sh​a​Psh​b​𝑑μ​𝑑s.\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{D}fP_{t-s}^{h}a\,P_{s}^{h}b\,d\mu\,ds.(9.44)

ForfN=(−N)∨(f∧N)f_{N}=(-N)\vee(f\wedge N),supr≥0‖(f−fN)​Prh​a‖2≤‖a‖∞​‖f−fN‖2⟶0,\sup_{r\geq 0}\|(f-f_{N})P_{r}^{h}a\|_{2}\leq\|a\|_{\infty}\|f-f_{N}\|_{2}\longrightarrow 0,

sor↦f​Prh​ar\mapsto fP_{r}^{h}aisL2​(μ)L^{2}(\mu)-continuous. Hencet↦ℭth​(a,b;f)t\mapsto\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)is continuous and|ℭth​(a,b;f)|≤t​‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞​∫D|f|​𝑑μ.|\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)|\leq t\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}\int_{D}|f|\,d\mu.(9.45)

The bound above givesExt+⁡ℭh​(a,b;f)∈𝒮+′​(ℝ)\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{h}(a,b;f)\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathbb{R}). Sinceℭ0h​(a,b;f)=0\mathfrak{C}_{0}^{h}(a,b;f)=0, the distributional derivative of this zero extension
has no jump boundary term att=0t=0.

## Lemma 9.8.

For everyλ>0\lambda>0,∫0∞e−λ​t​ℭth​(a,b;f)​𝑑t=∫Df​Rλh​a​Rλh​b​𝑑μ.\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)\,dt=\int_{D}f\,R_{\lambda}^{h}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\,d\mu.(9.46)

Consequently,ℒ​[∂t(Ext+⁡ℭh​(a,b;f))]​(λ)=λ​∫Df​Rλh​a​Rλh​b​𝑑μ.\mathscr{L}\!\left[\partial_{t}\bigl(\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{h}(a,b;f)\bigr)\right](\lambda)=\lambda\int_{D}f\,R_{\lambda}^{h}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\,d\mu.(9.47)

## Proof.

By (9.45),∫0∞e−λ​t​|ℭth​(a,b;f)|​𝑑t≤λ−2​‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞​∫D|f|​𝑑μ<∞.\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}|\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)|\,dt\leq\lambda^{-2}\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}\int_{D}|f|\,d\mu<\infty.

More precisely,∫0∞e−λ​t​∫0t∫D|f|​|Pt−sh​a|​|Psh​b|​𝑑μ​𝑑s​𝑑t≤λ−2​‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞​∫D|f|​𝑑μ.\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{D}|f|\,|P_{t-s}^{h}a|\,|P_{s}^{h}b|\,d\mu\,ds\,dt\leq\lambda^{-2}\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}\int_{D}|f|\,d\mu.

Fubini’s theorem andt=r+st=r+stherefore give∫0∞e−λ​t​ℭth​(a,b;f)​𝑑t=∫0∞∫0∞e−λ​(r+s)​⟨f​Prh​a,Psh​b⟩L2​(D,μ)​𝑑r​𝑑s.\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)\,dt=\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda(r+s)}\langle fP_{r}^{h}a,P_{s}^{h}b\rangle_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}\,dr\,ds.(9.48)

The mapr↦e−λ​r​f​Prh​ar\mapsto e^{-\lambda r}fP_{r}^{h}ais Bochner integrable inL2​(D,μ)L^{2}(D,\mu), since∫0∞e−λ​r​‖f​Prh​a‖L2​(D,μ)​𝑑r≤λ−1​‖a‖∞​‖f‖L2​(D,μ).\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda r}\|fP_{r}^{h}a\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}\,dr\leq\lambda^{-1}\|a\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}.

Moreover,∫0∞e−λ​r​f​Prh​a​𝑑r=f​Rλh​ain​L2​(D,μ).\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda r}fP_{r}^{h}a\,dr=fR_{\lambda}^{h}a\quad\text{in }L^{2}(D,\mu).(9.49)

Indeed, both sides belong toL2​(D,μ)L^{2}(D,\mu), and for every boundedg∈L2​(D,μ)g\in L^{2}(D,\mu),⟨∫0∞e−λ​r​f​Prh​a​𝑑r,g⟩\displaystyle\left\langle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda r}fP_{r}^{h}a\,dr,g\right\rangle=∫0∞e−λ​r​⟨Prh​a,f​g⟩​𝑑r\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda r}\langle P_{r}^{h}a,fg\rangle\,dr=⟨Rλh​a,f​g⟩=⟨f​Rλh​a,g⟩.\displaystyle=\langle R_{\lambda}^{h}a,fg\rangle=\langle fR_{\lambda}^{h}a,g\rangle.

Suchggare dense inL2​(D,μ)L^{2}(D,\mu)becauseμ​(D)<∞\mu(D)<\infty, proving
(9.49). Substituting it into
(9.48) and using
(9.4) once more yields∫0∞e−λ​t​ℭth​(a,b;f)​𝑑t\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\mathfrak{C}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)\,dt=∫0∞e−λ​s​⟨f​Rλh​a,Psh​b⟩L2​(D,μ)​𝑑s\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda s}\langle fR_{\lambda}^{h}a,P_{s}^{h}b\rangle_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}\,ds=⟨f​Rλh​a,Rλh​b⟩L2​(D,μ).\displaystyle=\langle fR_{\lambda}^{h}a,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\rangle_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}.

This proves (9.46).
Equation (9.47) follows from
(9.43).
∎

Define the endpoint termℌth​(a,b;f):=∫Df​(a​Pth​b+b​Pth​a)​𝑑μ,t≥0.\mathfrak{H}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f):=\int_{D}f\bigl(aP_{t}^{h}b+bP_{t}^{h}a\bigr)\,d\mu,\qquad t\geq 0.

It is continuous, sinceℌth​(a,b;f)=⟨f​a,Pth​b⟩L2​(D,μ)+⟨f​b,Pth​a⟩L2​(D,μ),\mathfrak{H}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)=\langle fa,P_{t}^{h}b\rangle_{L^{2}(D,\mu)}+\langle fb,P_{t}^{h}a\rangle_{L^{2}(D,\mu)},

andf​a,f​b∈L2​(D,μ)fa,fb\in L^{2}(D,\mu). It also satisfies|ℌth​(a,b;f)|≤2​‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞​∫D|f|​𝑑μ.|\mathfrak{H}_{t}^{h}(a,b;f)|\leq 2\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}\int_{D}|f|\,d\mu.

Set𝒲h​(a,b;f)\displaystyle\mathscr{W}^{h}(a,b;f):=γ​Ext+⁡ℌh​(a,b;f)−γ​∂t(Ext+⁡ℭh​(a,b;f)),\displaystyle:=\gamma\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{H}^{h}(a,b;f)-\gamma\partial_{t}\bigl(\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{h}(a,b;f)\bigr),(9.50)𝒦h​(a,b;f)\displaystyle\mathscr{K}^{h}(a,b;f):=−γ​∂t(Ext+⁡ℭh​(a,b;f)).\displaystyle:=-\gamma\partial_{t}\bigl(\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{C}^{h}(a,b;f)\bigr).(9.51)

The preceding bounds show that𝒲h​(a,b;f),𝒦h​(a,b;f)∈𝒮+′​(ℝ)\mathscr{W}^{h}(a,b;f),\mathscr{K}^{h}(a,b;f)\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathbb{R}).

## Proposition 9.9.

Fora,b∈ℬb​(D)a,b\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D)and everyλ>0\lambda>0,ℒ​[𝒲h​(a,b;f)]​(λ)\displaystyle\mathscr{L}[\mathscr{W}^{h}(a,b;f)](\lambda)=γ​∫Df​(a​Rλh​b+b​Rλh​a−λ​Rλh​a​Rλh​b)​𝑑μ,\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{D}f\bigl(aR_{\lambda}^{h}b+bR_{\lambda}^{h}a-\lambda R_{\lambda}^{h}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\bigr)\,d\mu,(9.52)ℒ​[𝒦h​(a,b;f)]​(λ)\displaystyle\mathscr{L}[\mathscr{K}^{h}(a,b;f)](\lambda)=−γ​λ​∫Df​Rλh​a​Rλh​b​𝑑μ.\displaystyle=-\gamma\lambda\int_{D}fR_{\lambda}^{h}aR_{\lambda}^{h}b\,d\mu.(9.53)

Together with (9.22) and
(9.35), these identities giveℒ​[𝒲h​(a,b;f)]​(λ)\displaystyle\mathscr{L}[\mathscr{W}^{h}(a,b;f)](\lambda)=∂τ𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)|τ=0,\displaystyle=\left.\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)\right|_{\tau=0},(9.54)ℒ​[𝒦h​(a,b;f)]​(λ)\displaystyle\mathscr{L}[\mathscr{K}^{h}(a,b;f)](\lambda)=∂τ𝒥λ,τ​(a,b)|τ=0.\displaystyle=\left.\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)\right|_{\tau=0}.

Thus𝒲h​(a,b;f)\mathscr{W}^{h}(a,b;f)and𝒦h​(a,b;f)\mathscr{K}^{h}(a,b;f)are the unique causal
representatives of the moving-measure and fixed-base resolvent responses,
respectively. These are only Laplace-transform identities; no convergence of a
time-domain heat-semigroup difference quotient in𝒮′​(ℝ)\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})is
asserted.

## Proof.

The endpoint terms are absolutely Laplace integrable, since∫0∞e−λ​t​|∫Df​a​Pth​b​𝑑μ|​𝑑t≤λ−1​‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞​∫D|f|​𝑑μ,\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\left|\int_{D}faP_{t}^{h}b\,d\mu\right|dt\leq\lambda^{-1}\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}\int_{D}|f|\,d\mu,

and the same estimate holds after interchangingaaandbb. Hence Fubini’s
theorem and (9.4) yieldℒ​[Ext+⁡ℌh​(a,b;f)]​(λ)\displaystyle\mathscr{L}[\operatorname{Ext}_{+}\mathfrak{H}^{h}(a,b;f)](\lambda)=∫Df​(a​Rλh​b+b​Rλh​a)​𝑑μ.\displaystyle=\int_{D}f\bigl(aR_{\lambda}^{h}b+bR_{\lambda}^{h}a\bigr)\,d\mu.

Combining this identity with
Lemma9.8proves
(9.52) and
(9.53).
Equation (9.54) follows
from (9.22) and
(9.35). Since these identities hold for
everyλ>0\lambda>0, uniqueness follows from the injectivity of the Laplace
transform on𝒮+′​(ℝ)\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathbb{R}).
∎

## 10Averaged resolvent responses and absolute continuity

The moving-measure Ward identity yields a pathwise response measure. We prove
that it has finite energy, identify its Green potential with the Gaussian
Sobolev gradient, and deduce absolute continuity from response-Gram
non-degeneracy.

Fora,b∈ℬb​(D)a,b\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D)andλ>0\lambda>0, define the canonical Borel observableJλa,b​(h)=∫Da​Rλh​b​𝑑Mh.J_{\lambda}^{a,b}(h)=\int_{D}aR_{\lambda}^{h}b\,dM_{h}.(10.1)

Forf∈ℋregf\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}and every fixedτ\tau, Cameron–Martin covariance givesJλa,b​(h+τ​f)=𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)almost surely.J_{\lambda}^{a,b}(h+\tau f)=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)\qquad\text{almost surely}.

Thus𝒬λ,τ​(a,b)\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b)is the coherent representative of the
weak-shift response ofJλa,bJ_{\lambda}^{a,b}. Only this fixed-τ\tauidentification
is used below; the fixed-base pairing𝒥λ,τ\mathcal{J}_{\lambda,\tau}is a different
observable because it freezes both endpoint measures atMhM_{h}. The response
measure ofJλa,bJ_{\lambda}^{a,b}has finite Green energy throughout the subcritical
range. When0<γ<20<\gamma<\sqrt{2}, this energy is integrable, the potential is the Gaussian
Sobolev gradient, and the response Gram yields density statements; see
Remark2.1.

## 10.1Response measures and annealed energy

Letℳfin​(D)\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}(D)carry the sigma-field generated byν↦∫Dφ​𝑑ν\nu\mapsto\int_{D}\varphi\,d\nu,φ∈Cc​(D)\varphi\in C_{c}(D).

## Lemma 10.1.

The Jordan-decomposition mapsν⟼|ν|,ν⟼ν+,ν⟼ν−\nu\longmapsto|\nu|,\qquad\nu\longmapsto\nu^{+},\qquad\nu\longmapsto\nu^{-}

fromℳfin​(D)\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}(D)toℳfin+​(D)\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{+}(D)are measurable. Hence,
ifω↦σω\omega\mapsto\sigma^{\omega}is measurable through allCc​(D)C_{c}(D)-pairings,
then(σω)+(\sigma^{\omega})^{+}and(σω)−(\sigma^{\omega})^{-}are measurable positive
kernels. Moreover, the mapsℳfin+​(D)2∋(μ1,μ2)\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{+}(D)^{2}\ni(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})⟼ℐD​(μ1,μ2),\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}),ℳfin​(D)∋ν\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}(D)\ni\nu⟼ℐD​(ν+)+ℐD​(ν−)\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu^{+})+\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu^{-})

are[0,∞][0,\infty]-valued and measurable. In particular,𝔐ℰ​(D)\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D)is measurable, andν↦ℐD​(ν)\nu\mapsto\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu)is measurable on𝔐ℰ​(D)\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D).

## Proof.

Choose compact setsKr⋐DK_{r}\Subset Dincreasing toDD. By separability and the
Tietze extension theorem, for everyrrthere is a countable set𝒜r⊂{g∈Cc​(D):‖g‖∞≤1}\mathscr{A}_{r}\subset\{g\in C_{c}(D):\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}

whose restrictions are uniformly dense in the unit ball ofC​(Kr)C(K_{r}). If0≤φ∈Cc​(D)0\leq\varphi\in C_{c}(D)andsupp⁡φ⊂Kr\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset K_{r}, the dual
characterization of total variation gives∫Dφ​d​|ν|=supg∈𝒜r∫Dφ​g​𝑑ν.\int_{D}\varphi\,d|\nu|=\sup_{g\in\mathscr{A}_{r}}\int_{D}\varphi g\,d\nu.(10.2)

The right-hand side is a countable supremum of measurable functions. Thereforeν↦∫φ​d​|ν|\nu\mapsto\int\varphi\,d|\nu|is measurable, and∫Dφ​𝑑ν±=12​(∫Dφ​d​|ν|±∫Dφ​𝑑ν).\int_{D}\varphi\,d\nu^{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{D}\varphi\,d|\nu|\pm\int_{D}\varphi\,d\nu\right).

First treating non-negativeφ\varphiand then taking linear combinations proves
measurability through allCc​(D)C_{c}(D)-pairings. A monotone-class argument extends the
kernel assertion to Borel sets.

The truncation and monotone-class argument in
Lemma7.1, now applied to two measure
variables, shows that(μ1,μ2)↦ℐD​(μ1,μ2)(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\mapsto\mathscr{I}_{D}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})is Borel.
The remaining assertions follow from the measurable Jordan decomposition, the
definition of𝔐ℰ​(D)\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D), and the four-term formula in
Lemma4.5.
∎

## Definition 10.2.

A random finite-energy signed Radon measure is a measurable mapω↦σω∈ℳfin​(D)\omega\mapsto\sigma^{\omega}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}(D)such thatℐD​((σω)+)+ℐD​((σω)−)<∞almost surely.\mathscr{I}_{D}((\sigma^{\omega})^{+})+\mathscr{I}_{D}((\sigma^{\omega})^{-})<\infty\quad\text{almost surely}.(10.3)

## Lemma 10.3.

Ifσ\sigmais a random finite-energy signed Radon measure with𝔼​ℐD​(σ)<∞\mathbb{E}\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma)<\infty, then𝒰D​σ\mathscr{U}_{D}\sigmahas a strongly
measurableℋ\mathcal{H}-valued versionVVsatisfyingV∈L2​(Ω;ℋ),𝔼​‖V‖ℋ2=𝔼​ℐD​(σ).V\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}),\qquad\mathbb{E}\|V\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma).(10.4)

## Proof.

LetAσ:={ℐD​(σ+)+ℐD​(σ−)<∞}.A_{\sigma}:=\{\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma^{+})+\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma^{-})<\infty\}.

This event is measurable by Lemma10.1and has
probability one. Replacingσ\sigmaby𝟏Aσ​σ\mathbf{1}_{A_{\sigma}}\sigma, which does not change
it almost surely, we may assume thatσ​(ω)∈𝔐ℰ​(D)\sigma(\omega)\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D)for everyω\omega. PutV=𝒰D​σV=\mathscr{U}_{D}\sigmaand choose a countable total family(fj)j≥1⊂Cc∞​(D)(f_{j})_{j\geq 1}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(D)inℋ\mathcal{H}. By
Lemma4.5,⟨V,fj⟩ℋ=∫Dfj​𝑑σ,\langle V,f_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\int_{D}f_{j}\,d\sigma,

which is measurable for everyjj. Finite linear combinations of thefjf_{j}are
dense inℋ\mathcal{H}, so pointwise approximation gives measurability ofω↦⟨V​(ω),f⟩ℋ\omega\mapsto\langle V(\omega),f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}for everyf∈ℋf\in\mathcal{H}. ThusVVis
weakly measurable; separability ofℋ\mathcal{H}and Pettis’ theorem give a strongly
measurable version. The norm identity
(4.11) gives
(10.4).
∎

LetCb∞​(ℝn)C_{b}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})denote the smooth functions whose partial

class𝒞cyl∞:={Φ​(W​(f1),…,W​(fn)):n≥1,fi∈ℋ,Φ∈Cb∞​(ℝn)}.\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty}:=\left\{\Phi(W(f_{1}),\ldots,W(f_{n})):n\geq 1,\ f_{i}\in\mathcal{H},\ \Phi\in C_{b}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\right\}.(10.5)

and, for everyZ∈𝒞cyl∞Z\in\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty},𝔼​[Z​F​(h+τ​f)−F​(h)τ]⟶𝔼​[Z​∫Df​𝑑σFh]\mathbb{E}\!\left[Z\frac{F(h+\tau f)-F(h)}{\tau}\right]\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}\!\left[Z\int_{D}f\,d\sigma_{F}^{h}\right](10.8)

where both limits are taken asτ→0\tau\to 0.

LetF=(F1,…,Fm):𝖷→ℝmF=(F_{1},\ldots,F_{m}):\mathsf{X}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}be Borel withF​(h)∈L2​(Ω;ℝm)F(h)\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{m}). Suppose eachFiF_{i}has response measureσih\sigma_{i}^{h}and𝔼​∑i=1mℐD​(σih)<∞.\mathbb{E}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma_{i}^{h})<\infty.(10.9)

and𝐂FG​(i,j)=⟨DG​Fi,DG​Fj⟩ℋ=ℐD​(σih,σjh).\mathbf{C}_{F}^{\mathrm{G}}(i,j)=\langle D^{\mathrm{G}}F_{i},D^{\mathrm{G}}F_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma_{i}^{h},\sigma_{j}^{h}).(10.11)

Ifdet𝐂FG>0\det\mathbf{C}_{F}^{\mathrm{G}}>0almost surely, thenF#​ℙ≪ℒm.F_{\#}\mathbb{P}\ll\mathcal{L}^{m}.(10.12)

Taking𝒱=ℋreg\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{reg}}, which is dense inℋ\mathcal{H},
Definition10.4supplies the
weak-shift hypotheses of CorollaryA.1componentwise; hence
(10.10) holds. Equation
(10.11) follows from

## Lemma 10.6.

Assume0<γ<20<\gamma<\sqrt{2}. Then𝔼​ℐD​(Mh)<∞,𝔼​[Mh​(D)2]<∞.\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{I}_{D}(M_{h})<\infty,\qquad\mathbb{E}[M_{h}(D)^{2}]<\infty.(10.13)

## Proof.

In the second-moment rangeγ2<2\gamma^{2}<2, the GMC second-moment measure is𝔼​[Mh​(d​x)​Mh​(d​y)]=eγ2​GD​(x,y)​d​x​d​y;\mathbb{E}[M_{h}(dx)M_{h}(dy)]=e^{\gamma^{2}G_{D}(x,y)}\,dx\,dy;(10.14)

see[15,20]. For every bounded open domain,0≤GD​(x,y)≤CD+log+⁡1|x−y|;0\leq G_{D}(x,y)\leq C_{D}+\log^{+}\frac{1}{|x-y|};(10.15)

this follows from the disk comparison in
Lemma3.10. Tonelli’s theorem therefore gives𝔼​ℐD​(Mh)\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{I}_{D}(M_{h})=∬D2GD​(x,y)​eγ2​GD​(x,y)​𝑑x​𝑑y\displaystyle=\iint_{D^{2}}G_{D}(x,y)e^{\gamma^{2}G_{D}(x,y)}\,dx\,dy≤C​∬D2(1+log+⁡1|x−y|)​|x−y|−γ2​𝑑x​𝑑y<∞,\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{D^{2}}\left(1+\log^{+}\frac{1}{|x-y|}\right)|x-y|^{-\gamma^{2}}\,dx\,dy<\infty,

because∫0diam⁡D(1+|log⁡r|)​r1−γ2​𝑑r<∞⟺γ2<2.\int_{0}^{\operatorname{diam}D}(1+|\log r|)r^{1-\gamma^{2}}\,dr<\infty\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\gamma^{2}<2.

The same calculation without the factorGDG_{D}proves𝔼​[Mh​(D)2]<∞\mathbb{E}[M_{h}(D)^{2}]<\infty.
∎

## 10.2Averaged resolvent response

## Lemma 10.7.

Leta,b,c,d∈ℬb​(D)a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{B}_{b}(D)be real-valued. Forλ>0\lambda>0, setΘλa,b,h\displaystyle\Theta_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}:=γ​(a​Rλh​b+b​Rλh​a−λ​Rλh​a​Rλh​b),\displaystyle:=\gamma\bigl(aR_{\lambda}^{h}b+bR_{\lambda}^{h}a-\lambda R_{\lambda}^{h}a\,R_{\lambda}^{h}b\bigr),(10.16)σλa,b,h\displaystyle\sigma_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}:=Θλa,b,h​Mh.\displaystyle:=\Theta_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}M_{h}.(10.17)

Then(h,λ)↦σλa,b,h(h,\lambda)\mapsto\sigma_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}is a measurable finite signed
Radon kernel and‖Θλa,b,h‖∞≤3​γλ​‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞,\|\Theta_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{3\gamma}{\lambda}\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty},(10.18)

and‖σλa,b,h‖TV≤3​γλ​‖a‖∞​‖b‖∞​Mh​(D).\|\sigma_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}\|_{\rm TV}\leq\frac{3\gamma}{\lambda}\|a\|_{\infty}\|b\|_{\infty}M_{h}(D).(10.19)

Moreover, forλ,λ′>0\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}>0,ℐλ,λ′a,b;c,d​(h):=⟨Θλa,b,h,𝒢h​Θλ′c,d,h⟩L2​(Mh)\mathscr{I}_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}^{a,b;c,d}(h):=\langle\Theta_{\lambda}^{a,b,h},\mathscr{G}_{h}\Theta_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{c,d,h}\rangle_{L^{2}(M_{h})}(10.20)

is jointly Borel in(h,λ,λ′)(h,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})and, onΩop\Omega_{\rm op},ℐλ,λ′a,b;c,d​(h)=ℐD​(σλa,b,h,σλ′c,d,h).\mathscr{I}_{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}^{a,b;c,d}(h)=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma_{\lambda}^{a,b,h},\sigma_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{c,d,h}).(10.21)

## Proof.

For fixed bounded Borelgg,sg​(Mh)=[g]Mhs_{g}(M_{h})=[g]_{M_{h}}is a measurable section. Henceug,λ:=Rλh​sg​(Mh)u_{g,\lambda}:=R_{\lambda}^{h}s_{g}(M_{h})is jointly measurable. OnΩop\Omega_{\rm op}the
Markov resolvent estimate gives‖ug,λ‖∞≤λ−1​‖g‖∞,\|u_{g,\lambda}\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda^{-1}\|g\|_{\infty},

whileug,λ=0u_{g,\lambda}=0offΩop\Omega_{\rm op}. Thus products areL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h})-valued
measurable sections: forφ∈Cc​(D)\varphi\in C_{c}(D),∫Dφ​ua,λ​ub,λ​𝑑Mh=⟨Multφ⁡ua,λ,ub,λ⟩L2​(Mh).\int_{D}\varphi u_{a,\lambda}u_{b,\lambda}\,dM_{h}=\langle\operatorname{Mult}_{\varphi}u_{a,\lambda},u_{b,\lambda}\rangle_{L^{2}(M_{h})}.

The remaining terms satisfy∫Dφ​a​ub,λ​𝑑Mh=⟨sφ​a​(Mh),ub,λ⟩,∫Dφ​b​ua,λ​𝑑Mh=⟨sφ​b​(Mh),ua,λ⟩,\int_{D}\varphi au_{b,\lambda}\,dM_{h}=\langle s_{\varphi a}(M_{h}),u_{b,\lambda}\rangle,\qquad\int_{D}\varphi bu_{a,\lambda}\,dM_{h}=\langle s_{\varphi b}(M_{h}),u_{a,\lambda}\rangle,

and are Borel as well. Therefore(h,λ)⟼∫Dφ​Θλa,b,h​𝑑Mh(h,\lambda)\longmapsto\int_{D}\varphi\Theta_{\lambda}^{a,b,h}\,dM_{h}

is Borel for everyφ∈Cc​(D)\varphi\in C_{c}(D), which is measurability of the signed-measure
map. Lemma10.1gives measurable Jordan kernels.

The Markov estimate gives
(10.18), which in turn proves
(10.19). Finally,
measurability of the sections, of𝒢h\mathscr{G}_{h}, and of the Hilbert-field inner product gives
joint Borel measurability in (10.20);
(10.21) follows from
(4.34) onΩop\Omega_{\rm op}.
∎

For everyτ\tau, the coherent pairing satisfiesJ~τ​(h)=𝒬λ,τ​(a,b).\widetilde{J}_{\tau}(h)=\mathcal{Q}_{\lambda,\tau}(a,b).

which is (10.24) in the coherent-line sense.

Substitution in (10.29) yields|J~τ−J~0τ|≤Ca,b,λ,f​Mh​(D),0<|τ|≤1.\left|\frac{\widetilde{J}_{\tau}-\widetilde{J}_{0}}{\tau}\right|\leq C_{a,b,\lambda,f}M_{h}(D),\qquad 0<|\tau|\leq 1.(10.30)

With𝖣𝝀:=diag⁡(λ1γ,…,λmγ),\mathsf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}:=\operatorname{diag}\left(\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\gamma},\ldots,\frac{\lambda_{m}}{\gamma}\right),𝐂^h,𝝀resp=𝖣𝝀​𝐂h,𝝀resp​𝖣𝝀,det𝐂^h,𝝀resp=(γ−m​∏i=1mλi)2​det𝐂h,𝝀resp.\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{h,\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{resp}}=\mathsf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mathbf{C}_{h,\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{resp}}\mathsf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},\qquad\det\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{h,\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{resp}}=\left(\gamma^{-m}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\right)^{2}\det\mathbf{C}_{h,\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{resp}}.(10.47)

## Lemma 10.12.

For everyh∈Ωoph\in\Omega_{\rm op},𝝀⟼det𝐂^h,𝝀resp\boldsymbol{\lambda}\longmapsto\det\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{h,\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{resp}}(10.48)

is real analytic on(0,∞)m(0,\infty)^{m}. The map(h,𝝀)⟼det𝐂^h,𝝀resp(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})\longmapsto\det\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{h,\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{resp}}(10.49)

is jointly Borel on𝖷×(0,∞)m\mathsf{X}\times(0,\infty)^{m}and is non-negative.

## Proof.

Fixh∈Ωoph\in\Omega_{\rm op}andλ0>0\lambda_{0}>0. SetB=L2​(Mh)∩L∞​(Mh)B=L^{2}(M_{h})\cap L^{\infty}(M_{h})with‖u‖B=‖u‖2+‖u‖∞\|u\|_{B}=\|u\|_{2}+\|u\|_{\infty}. For|λ−λ0|<λ0/2|\lambda-\lambda_{0}|<\lambda_{0}/2,Rλh=Rλ0h​∑n=0∞[−(λ−λ0)​Rλ0h]nin​ℒ​(B),R_{\lambda}^{h}=R_{\lambda_{0}}^{h}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}[-(\lambda-\lambda_{0})R_{\lambda_{0}}^{h}]^{n}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{L}(B),

because‖Rλ0h‖L2→L2,‖Rλ0h‖L∞→L∞≤λ0−1\|R_{\lambda_{0}}^{h}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}},\|R_{\lambda_{0}}^{h}\|_{L^{\infty}\to L^{\infty}}\leq\lambda_{0}^{-1}.
Thusλi↦xi​(h,𝝀)\lambda_{i}\mapsto x_{i}(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})is analytic inBB. Since‖u​v‖L2​(Mh)≤‖u‖∞​‖v‖L2​(Mh),\|uv\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})}\leq\|u\|_{\infty}\|v\|_{L^{2}(M_{h})},

λi↦Θ^i​(h,𝝀)\lambda_{i}\mapsto\widehat{\Theta}_{i}(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})is analytic inL2​(Mh)L^{2}(M_{h}). Every Gram entry is a continuous bilinear expression, hence the entries
and determinant are real analytic.

For the Borel assertion, observe thatΘ^i​(h,𝝀)​Mh=λiγ​σλiψi,ψi,h.\widehat{\Theta}_{i}(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})M_{h}=\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\gamma}\sigma_{\lambda_{i}}^{\psi_{i},\psi_{i},h}.

Therefore𝐂^h,𝝀resp​(i,j)=λi​λjγ2​ℐλi,λjψi,ψi;ψj,ψj​(h),\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{h,\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{resp}}(i,j)=\frac{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}{\gamma^{2}}\mathscr{I}_{\lambda_{i},\lambda_{j}}^{\psi_{i},\psi_{i};\psi_{j},\psi_{j}}(h),

and joint Borel measurability follows from

entrywise whenevermini⁡λi→∞\min_{i}\lambda_{i}\to\infty, anddet𝐂^h,∞resp>0.\det\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{h,\infty}^{\mathrm{resp}}>0.(10.52)

SinceΘ^i​(h,𝝀)−ψi2=−(xi​(h,𝝀)−ψi)2,\widehat{\Theta}_{i}(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})-\psi_{i}^{2}=-\bigl(x_{i}(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})-\psi_{i}\bigr)^{2},

we have‖Θ^i​(h,𝝀)−ψi2‖2≤2​‖ψi‖∞​‖xi​(h,𝝀)−ψi‖2⟶0.\|\widehat{\Theta}_{i}(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})-\psi_{i}^{2}\|_{2}\leq 2\|\psi_{i}\|_{\infty}\|x_{i}(h,\boldsymbol{\lambda})-\psi_{i}\|_{2}\longrightarrow 0.(10.53)

Continuity of(u,v)↦⟨u,𝒢h​v⟩(u,v)\mapsto\langle u,\mathscr{G}_{h}v\rangleonL2​(Mh)2L^{2}(M_{h})^{2}yields
(10.51).

Forc∈ℝm∖{0}c\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\setminus\{0\}, setνc=∑ici​ψi2​Mh\nu_{c}=\sum_{i}c_{i}\psi_{i}^{2}M_{h}. Chooseiiwithci≠0c_{i}\neq 0and a pointzi∈Dz_{i}\in Dwithψi​(zi)>0\psi_{i}(z_{i})>0. The setVi:={z∈D:ψi​(z)>12​ψi​(zi)}V_{i}:=\{z\in D:\psi_{i}(z)>\tfrac{1}{2}\psi_{i}(z_{i})\}

is non-empty and open. Disjointness of the supports givesνc|Vi=ci​ψi2​Mh|Vi,|νc|​(Vi)≥|ci|​ψi​(zi)24​Mh​(Vi)>0\nu_{c}|_{V_{i}}=c_{i}\psi_{i}^{2}M_{h}|_{V_{i}},\qquad|\nu_{c}|(V_{i})\geq|c_{i}|\frac{\psi_{i}(z_{i})^{2}}{4}M_{h}(V_{i})>0

by full support ofMhM_{h}. Henceνc≠0\nu_{c}\neq 0. Since∑ici​ψi2∈L2​(Mh)\sum_{i}c_{i}\psi_{i}^{2}\in L^{2}(M_{h}),
Lemma4.8givesνc∈𝔐ℰ​(D)\nu_{c}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{E}}(D). Lemma4.6then givescT​𝐂^h,∞resp​c=ℐD​(νc)>0.c^{T}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{h,\infty}^{\mathrm{resp}}c=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\nu_{c})>0.

## Lemma 10.14.

LetU⊂ℝdU\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}be connected and open, and letΦ:Ω×U→[0,∞)\Phi:\Omega\times U\to[0,\infty)be jointly measurable. Assume that, for almost
everyω\omega, the functiont↦Φ​(ω,t)t\mapsto\Phi(\omega,t)is real analytic and not
identically zero. ThenNΦ:={t∈U:ℙ​(Φ​(⋅,t)=0)>0}N_{\Phi}:=\{t\in U:\mathbb{P}(\Phi(\cdot,t)=0)>0\}(10.54)

is Borel and Lebesgue-null, andΦ​(⋅,t)>0\Phi(\cdot,t)>0almost surely for everyt∈U∖NΦt\in U\setminus N_{\Phi}.

## Proof.

HenceNΦ={p>0}N_{\Phi}=\{p>0\}is Borel and null. SinceΦ≥0\Phi\geq 0,p​(t)=0p(t)=0impliesΦ​(⋅,t)>0\Phi(\cdot,t)>0almost surely.
∎

## Appendix AGaussian Sobolev realization of measure-valued field responses

This appendix supplies the Gaussian Sobolev closure used in
Section10:σh​represents the weak shift response of​F⟹DG​F=𝒰D​σh,\sigma^{h}\text{ represents the weak shift response of }F\quad\Longrightarrow\quad D^{\mathrm{G}}F=\mathscr{U}_{D}\sigma^{h},

and records the Bouleau–Hirsch density criterion. Neither result enters the
spectral argument of Part II.

## Standard Gaussian Sobolev facts.

On the canonical Gaussian realization of
Section2, letW:ℋ→L2​(Ω)W:\mathcal{H}\to L^{2}(\Omega)be the isonormal coordinate field defined in
(5.11), and let𝒜:=σ​{W​(f):f∈ℋ}ℙ=ℬ​(𝖷)¯ℙ.\mathscr{A}:=\sigma\{W(f):f\in\mathcal{H}\}^{\mathbb{P}}=\overline{\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{X})}^{\mathbb{P}}.

Indeed,W​(ι∗​ℓ)=ℓ​(h)W(\iota^{*}\ell)=\ell(h)forℓ∈𝖷∗\ell\in\mathsf{X}^{*}, whileℬ​(𝖷)=σ​(𝖷∗)\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{X})=\sigma(\mathsf{X}^{*}); separability ofℋ\mathcal{H}and density ofι∗​𝖷∗\iota^{*}\mathsf{X}^{*}inℋ\mathcal{H}give the reverse inclusion after completion.
Recall the class𝒞cyl∞\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty}from
(10.5). ForZ=Φ​(W​(f1),…,W​(fn)),Φ∈Cb∞​(ℝn),f1,…,fn∈ℋ,Z=\Phi(W(f_{1}),\ldots,W(f_{n})),\qquad\Phi\in C_{b}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\in\mathcal{H},(A.1)

setD0​Z=∑i=1n∂iΦ​(W​(f1),…,W​(fn))​fi.D_{0}Z=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{i}\Phi(W(f_{1}),\ldots,W(f_{n}))f_{i}.(A.2)

Finite-dimensional Gaussian calculus makes (A.2)
representation-independent; together with the sigma-field identity above, it also
shows that𝒞cyl∞\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty}is dense inL2​(Ω)L^{2}(\Omega).
The Gaussian integration-by-parts identity shows directly thatD0D_{0}is
closable. Indeed, ifZk→0Z_{k}\to 0inL2​(Ω)L^{2}(\Omega)andD0​Zk→VD_{0}Z_{k}\to VinL2​(Ω;ℋ)L^{2}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}), then, for everyZ∈𝒞cyl∞Z\in\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty}andg∈ℋg\in\mathcal{H},𝔼​[Z​⟨V,g⟩ℋ]=limk→∞𝔼​[Zk​{Z​W​(g)−⟨D0​Z,g⟩ℋ}]=0.\mathbb{E}[Z\langle V,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}]=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\!\left[Z_{k}\{ZW(g)-\langle D_{0}Z,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\}\right]=0.

Finite sums of fieldsZ​gZgare dense inL2​(Ω;ℋ)L^{2}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}), soV=0V=0. DefineDG:=D0¯,Dom​(DG)=𝔻1,2⊂L2​(Ω),D^{\mathrm{G}}:=\overline{D_{0}},\qquad\mathrm{Dom}(D^{\mathrm{G}})=\mathbb{D}^{1,2}\subset L^{2}(\Omega),(A.3)

and callDGD^{\mathrm{G}}the Gaussian Sobolev gradient. The standard weak-derivative
characterization of Gaussian Sobolev spaces[6, Definition 5.2.10 and Theorem 5.4.2], together
with the density ofι∗​𝖷∗\iota^{*}\mathsf{X}^{*}inℋ\mathcal{H}, gives, forF∈L2​(Ω)F\in L^{2}(\Omega)andV∈L2​(Ω;ℋ)V\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}),𝔼​[Z​⟨V,g⟩ℋ]=𝔼​[F​{Z​W​(g)−⟨D0​Z,g⟩ℋ}],∀Z∈𝒞cyl∞,g∈ℋ⟺F∈𝔻1,2,DG​F=V.\begin{gathered}\mathbb{E}[Z\langle V,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}]=\mathbb{E}\!\left[F\{ZW(g)-\langle D_{0}Z,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\}\right],\\[-2.84526pt]
\forall\,Z\in\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty},\quad g\in\mathcal{H}\end{gathered}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad F\in\mathbb{D}^{1,2},\qquad D^{\mathrm{G}}F=V.(A.4)

Equivalently, (A.4) is the duality
betweenDGD^{\mathrm{G}}and the divergence operator, applied toδ​(Z​g)=Z​W​(g)−⟨D0​Z,g⟩ℋ.\delta(Zg)=ZW(g)-\langle D_{0}Z,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

## Measure-valued response realization.

## Corollary A.1.

LetF:𝖷→ℝF:\mathsf{X}\to\mathbb{R}be Borel withF​(h)∈L2​(Ω)F(h)\in L^{2}(\Omega). Let𝒱⊂ℋ\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{H}be a dense linear subspace, and letσh\sigma^{h}be a
random finite-energy signed Radon measure in the sense of
Definition10.2. Assume𝔼​ℐD​(σh)<∞,\mathbb{E}\,\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma^{h})<\infty,(A.5)

and, for everyf∈𝒱f\in\mathcal{V},‖F​(h+τ​f)−F​(h)‖L1​(Ω)⟶0\|F(h+\tau f)-F(h)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\longrightarrow 0(A.6)

asτ→0\tau\to 0. Assume also that, for everyf∈𝒱f\in\mathcal{V}andZ∈𝒞cyl∞Z\in\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty},𝔼​[Z​(F​(h+τ​f)−F​(h)τ−∫Df~​𝑑σh)]⟶0\mathbb{E}\!\left[Z\left(\frac{F(h+\tau f)-F(h)}{\tau}-\int_{D}\widetilde{f}\,d\sigma^{h}\right)\right]\longrightarrow 0(A.7)

asτ→0\tau\to 0.
ThenF∈𝔻1,2,DG​F=𝒰D​σh,‖DG​F‖ℋ2=ℐD​(σh)almost surely.F\in\mathbb{D}^{1,2},\qquad D^{\mathrm{G}}F=\mathscr{U}_{D}\sigma^{h},\qquad\|D^{\mathrm{G}}F\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma^{h})\quad\text{almost surely}.(A.8)

## Proof.

PutV=𝒰D​σhV=\mathscr{U}_{D}\sigma^{h}. Lemma10.3and the Green reproducing identity giveV∈L2​(Ω;ℋ),‖V‖ℋ2=ℐD​(σh),⟨V,f⟩ℋ=∫Df~​𝑑σh.V\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}),\qquad\|V\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\mathscr{I}_{D}(\sigma^{h}),\qquad\langle V,f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\int_{D}\widetilde{f}\,d\sigma^{h}.(A.9)

Fixf∈𝒱f\in\mathcal{V}andZ=Φ​(W​(g1),…,W​(gn))∈𝒞cyl∞Z=\Phi(W(g_{1}),\ldots,W(g_{n}))\in\mathcal{C}_{\rm cyl}^{\infty}. WithFτ:=F​(h+τ​f),Zτ:=Z​(h+τ​f),𝒵τ​(f):=exp⁡(τ​W​(f)−τ22​‖f‖ℋ2),F_{\tau}:=F(h+\tau f),\qquad Z_{\tau}:=Z(h+\tau f),\qquad\mathscr{Z}_{\tau}(f):=\exp\!\left(\tau W(f)-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\right),

the fixed representatives from
(5.10) and Taylor’s formula yieldZτ\displaystyle Z_{\tau}=Φ(W(g1)+τ⟨g1,f⟩ℋ,…,\displaystyle=\Phi\!\left(W(g_{1})+\tau\langle g_{1},f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},\ldots,\right.(A.10)W(gn)+τ⟨gn,f⟩ℋ)almost surely,\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\left.W(g_{n})+\tau\langle g_{n},f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right)\quad\text{almost surely},‖Zτ−Zτ−⟨D0​Z,f⟩ℋ‖∞\displaystyle\left\|\frac{Z_{\tau}-Z}{\tau}-\langle D_{0}Z,f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right\|_{\infty}⟶0.\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0.

The Cameron–Martin formula gives𝔼​[Fτ​Zτ]=𝔼​[F​Z​𝒵τ​(f)].\mathbb{E}[F_{\tau}Z_{\tau}]=\mathbb{E}[FZ\mathscr{Z}_{\tau}(f)].(A.11)

By (A.6),
(A.7), and
(A.10),𝔼​[Fτ​Zτ]−𝔼​[F​Z]τ\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[F_{\tau}Z_{\tau}]-\mathbb{E}[FZ]}{\tau}=𝔼​[Z​Fτ−Fτ]+𝔼​[Fτ​Zτ−Zτ]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[Z\frac{F_{\tau}-F}{\tau}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[F_{\tau}\frac{Z_{\tau}-Z}{\tau}\right]⟶𝔼​[Z​⟨V,f⟩ℋ]+𝔼​[F​⟨D0​Z,f⟩ℋ].\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}[Z\langle V,f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}]+\mathbb{E}[F\langle D_{0}Z,f\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}].(A.12)

Moreover,𝒵τ​(f)−1τ⟶W​(f)in​L2​(Ω),\frac{\mathscr{Z}_{\tau}(f)-1}{\tau}\longrightarrow W(f)\quad\text{in }L^{2}(\Omega),(A.13)

so (A.11) also gives𝔼​[Fτ​Zτ]−𝔼​[F​Z]τ=𝔼​[F​Z​𝒵τ​(f)−1τ]⟶𝔼​[F​Z​W​(f)].\frac{\mathbb{E}[F_{\tau}Z_{\tau}]-\mathbb{E}[FZ]}{\tau}=\mathbb{E}\!\left[FZ\frac{\mathscr{Z}_{\tau}(f)-1}{\tau}\right]\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}[FZW(f)].(A.14)

Comparing (A.12) and

Both sides are continuous inf∈ℋf\in\mathcal{H}; hence density of𝒱\mathcal{V}extends

Equation (A.4) and

## Density criterion.

## Theorem A.2.

LetF=(F1,…,Fm)F=(F_{1},\ldots,F_{m})withFi∈𝔻1,2F_{i}\in\mathbb{D}^{1,2}, and set𝐂FG=(⟨DG​Fi,DG​Fj⟩ℋ)i,j=1m.\mathbf{C}_{F}^{\mathrm{G}}=\bigl(\langle D^{\mathrm{G}}F_{i},D^{\mathrm{G}}F_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\bigr)_{i,j=1}^{m}.(A.16)

Ifdet𝐂FG>0\det\mathbf{C}_{F}^{\mathrm{G}}>0almost surely, thenF#​ℙ≪ℒm.F_{\#}\mathbb{P}\ll\mathcal{L}^{m}.(A.17)

This is the Bouleau–Hirsch criterion[7, Chapter II, Section 5]; see also[8].
